- 2026-08-01 | Feature | SB-2 image lineage: the anti-stale engine (D-#409/#417/#419). New BookImageAsset (one row per artifact per stage of a slot chain: APPROVED → CROPPED → UPSCALED → COMPLIANT), with each stage recording a fingerprint of the artifact it was DERIVED FROM — so re-approving an image makes every downstream row disagree with reality, and the disagreement IS the staleness; nothing has to remember to invalidate anything. A re-upload SUPERSEDES rather than overwrites, because an image a reviewer rejected is evidence. StoredFile gains four book kinds (book_image_raw/approved/compliant, book_pdf) and DriveStore now accepts a NESTED subfolder path plus Drive appProperties, so a file found from the Drive side names its own book/lesson/slot/stage instead of being an opaque blob. THE BUG THE TESTS CAUGHT, and it is the whole point of the slice: staleness did not CASCADE. Comparing each stage to its direct input only marks CROPPED stale — UPSCALED still matches the cropped FILE, which did not change, only its standing did — so a per-stage check reported UPSCALED and COMPLIANT as fresh and the stale picture would have sailed through to print, which is exactly the failure D-#417 exists to stop. chainStates() now walks the whole chain once and carries staleness downward. Also pinned: an artifact whose input VANISHED is stale (not fresh, which a naive compare reports because there is nothing to compare against), a MISSING stage does not make its downstream stale, and the stale report names FILES not stages. Gates: server+shared typecheck clean, jest 2630/2630 (156 suites, 14 new). | support-book-sb2
- 2026-08-01 | Feature | SB-2 upload route + slot workspace (D-#409/#417/#419). POST /files/book takes multipart artwork or a rendered edition, validates by kind (PNG/JPEG ≤ 25 MB, PDF ≤ 300 MB), uploads to Drive under books/<BOOK_ID>/<stage>/ with appProperties naming book/lesson/slot/stage, persists StoredFile, and REGISTERS the artifact on the same request — registering is what makes downstream stages stale, so it must never be a follow-up call a client could forget. Read gate: any holder of book:read may read any book file, because book production carries no student PII and there is nothing to row-scope BY; the real gate is that book:read is granted per user via AC-1. Three resolvers: the illustrator queue (prompt, refs, per-stage lineage, hasStale), the book-wide stale report, and a slot full history including superseded rows. compliance_note is never read by any resolver (README §5 — stripe language must not reach a prompt), and the guard asserts the ACCESS not the word, since the comments explaining the omission would trip a substring match. Second-order fix: the GET /files/:id dispatch first used the exported book-kind list and broke a dozen suites that mock the StoredFile module — it now dispatches on the book_ PREFIX, with a test pinning that invariant in both directions rather than assuming it. Gates: server+shared typecheck clean, vocab verifier PASS, jest 2650/2650 (157 suites, 14 new). | support-book-sb2

README §7 seven-item checklist becomes vocab and IS the sign-off — checklistPassed goes true only on APPROVE with every item ticked, since a partially-ticked list that still signs off makes the checklist decorative. Reviewer ≠ author enforced for everyone except a PRINCIPAL, whose round is stamped selfReviewed and carried onto the lesson (D-#424). Sign-off refuses while any escalation is unresolved, and ANSWERED counts as unresolved because someone still has to apply the ruling. A senior reply ANSWERS, a further reply re-OPENS, indefinitely. Two details: the round snapshots artifactPatchId at assignment so a re-merge mid-review is visible rather than silently changing what is under review, and isSenior on a reply is DERIVED from the caller grants rather than client-supplied, or a reviewer could mark their own escalation answered. Seven resolvers, four audit kinds, ReviewRuleError registered as expected. - 2026-08-01 | Feature | SB-4 assembly orchestration (D-#406/#407/#413/#417/#423). BookBuildJob + BookBuildService + BookRenderRunner + four resolvers. The gate runs BEFORE anything expensive and reports every reason at once — stale artifacts named by file, unresolved escalations (ANSWERED counts), empty scope — since fixing one blocker only to be told the next is the worst version of a gate; force is PRINCIPAL-only and recorded on the job and in the timeline. Concurrency 1 enforced by atomic claim rather than convention (the constraint is host memory and the VM has no swap), with requeueStuckJobs so a killed worker is recoverable instead of wedging the queue. materializeBookJson emits the SCHEMA snake_case names the frozen CLI reads, and doubles as the export escape hatch (D-#406). The runner spawns with an argument array and shell:false, runs validate FIRST so a book failing a JSON check never reaches Chromium, and fails the whole job if either edition fails. The spawn seam is INJECTED, so 24 tests pin the gate, queue, materialization and ordering without a browser. RenderError deleted as dead code — a failed render is a result, not an exception. NOT DONE: the pipeline is not yet vendored into /book-pipeline/ (BOOK_PIPELINE_ROOT points at it), no end-to-end render has run (needs VM Chromium + PUPPETEER_EXECUTABLE_PATH + python3-pil, verified under the systemd unit), and the worker loop + SSE endpoint are unwritten. - 2026-08-02 | Chore | VM prep for SB-4 (D-#434): installed python3-pil 10.2.0 and Chromium 150 (snap — the only form Ubuntu 24.04 aarch64 offers), then verified the assumption everyone skips: whether the browser can read what the renderer writes. IT CANNOT. A snap has a PRIVATE /tmp namespace, so file:///tmp/... — exactly where BookRenderRunner wrote its book folder via os.tmpdir() — fails to load, as an empty render or an opaque non-zero exit that never mentions namespaces. $HOME reads fine. Fix: BOOK_WORK_ROOT, defaulting to os.tmpdir() (correct anywhere unconfined) and pointed at /home/deploy/scdhub-book-work on the VM; three new tests pin the default, the override, and that the folder is actually created and cleaned under the root. Verified under systemd-run as the deploy user with the service env — the check D-#413 asked for, and the one that looks pedantic right up until it fails. Also recorded: the prod unit sets PrivateTmp=no and ProtectHome=no, so nothing compounds the snap confinement. NOT APPLIED: the two .env lines (PUPPETEER_EXECUTABLE_PATH=/snap/bin/chromium, BOOK_WORK_ROOT=/home/deploy/scdhub-book-work) — editing a running service env is a deploy act, not preparation. Gates: server+shared typecheck clean, jest 2717/2717 (160 suites, 3 new). | support-book-sb4
- 2026-08-02 | Feature | SB-4 worker loop + SSE log stream (D-#407/#418/#423). BookBuildWorker claims a job, materializes book.json, pulls the compliant images from Drive, renders, files each PDF as book_pdf under books/<id>/pdf, and marks the job; server/src/workers/bookBuildWorker.ts runs it as its own process (npm run worker:book). GET /book-builds/:jobId/stream serves the live log — and because the worker is a SEPARATE process it cannot publish into the API memory, so the log is appended with an atomic aggregation-pipeline $concat (a read-modify-write would drop chunks, onLog fires un-awaited) and the route tails that field; a reconnecting browser therefore gets the whole log from byte 0 and the log survives the worker dying. X-Accel-Buffering:no because Caddy and Cloudflare both buffer by default. THE BUG THE TESTS FOUND: a render that reported success but whose PDFs could not be read was marked SUCCEEDED with an empty outputs list — the assembler told the book was built with nothing to download. Now FAILS with a reason. It surfaced only because the first test runner PRINTED paths rather than writing files, leaving the upload half untested; the runner now writes real files so readPdf and upload actually execute. Also pinned: images keyed by the FILENAME book.json names (the renderer resolves filenames, not slot ids), a slot with no filename skipped because the validator owns that judgement, Drive-down recorded on the job so the queue keeps draining, and the work dir cleaned in a finally. Boot requeues stuck jobs; SIGTERM finishes the current job rather than killing a render mid-PDF. Gates: shared build, vocab verifier PASS, server+shared typecheck clean, jest 2731/2731 (161 suites, 14 new). NOT DONE: no end-to-end render yet — needs the two VM env lines, npm install in book-pipeline/, and a book with compliant images. | support-book-sb4

- 2026-08-02 | Feature | The Class-test dashboard drill-down is ordered by what needs DOING and paged at 50 (D-#430, owner ask). Order: complete-but-not-yet-visible-to-guardians (the release backlog) → incomplete AND overdue → incomplete → complete AND published. `state` is already a mutually-exclusive partition where overdue MEANS incomplete-and-past-deadline, so the four buckets cover every row exactly once; within a bucket, newest exam first, except overdue which leads with the most days late. Bucket 1 takes !publishedAt rather than the stricter Unpublished badge, so a complete test whose marks were never submitted sits with the work still to do. Pager: 50 a page, hidden when everything fits, index CLAMPED (not reset by an effect) so a filter that shrinks the list cannot strand an empty page; either filter returns to page 1. Rule lifted into app/src/lib/ctDashboardOrder.ts beside lib/ctPublishStatus. No server change. App typecheck clean; 18/18 executed assertions over synthetic rows (buckets, full sort order, total coverage, 123 rows → 50/50/23 pages, clamping). scdhub_local holds zero class-test exams, so the RENDERING is unverified locally — check it on the dev site after deploy. | ct-dashboard-order

Re-scoped after SB-1..SB-4: the PRD framing (build the read surface) was overtaken because BookEvent has been written since SB-1 and supportBookTimeline already read it. Three things were actually missing. (1) Per-ITEM timelines — the targetType/targetId index existed but nothing queried it, so "why does THIS BLOCK read this way" was unanswerable although the data was there; supportBookItemRationale returns it oldest-first, because a rationale is a story and a story told backwards is a list. (3) Actor names — BookActorService is the ONE place a book read crosses to identity: batched, by id only, at the resolver layer as D-#404 permits; it never throws, because a name lookup failing must not take down the timeline it decorates, and an unresolved id reads "(unknown account)" rather than blank since a deleted account is a real answer and an empty string renders as a gap that looks like a bug. Regression caught: the memo broke five merge tests that mock every book model — unmocked, the write buffers against an unopened connection and times out rather than failing fast. - 2026-08-02 | Feature | SB-6 in-app authoring chat, SERVER SIDE (D-#403/#408/#412). Scoped to the server on purpose: the owner uses Claude Desktop for the support-book JSON for the next few months, so an Expo chat UI would sit unused while ageing. The provider is a SEAM not a choice - which model writes Bengali well enough for a taught-letter inventory is empirical and the validator RED count is already a free objective scorer, so the model is config (BOOK_AUTHOR_MODEL) defaulting to the existing GEMINI_API_KEY; the seam follows CommentProvider (D-#399) including its hard-won details (an alias resolves to a dated model and that is what a bad batch is traced to; thought parts are reasoning, never the answer; a non-STOP finish is a truncated reply and is rejected). THE PROMPT ORDER IS THE FEATURE: policy first and byte-stable, then book, then lesson, then turn - caching is a strict PREFIX match, so a varying prefix still works and simply costs full price every turn, surfacing on an invoice months later rather than in a test; tests assert the prefix is byte-identical across turns, that the lesson is not in it, and that prefix hashes match. Policy is verbatim (a paraphrase drifts from what the Principal approved) and the preamble tells the model it is NOT the gate, since a model that believes its output ships argues with the validator instead of fixing the text. One write path holds (D-#408): a patch turn goes through the same submitPatch a Desktop upload uses, source IN_APP_CHAT recorded and branched on nowhere, a RED returns findings with the session left OPEN. Every turn records model, resolved model, policySetHash, prompt version and usage; a per-book monthly ceiling refuses in Bangla, checked before the turn so one turn may overshoot - deliberate, since cost is unknowable until the call returns. AuthorChatError registered as expected. NOT built: the Expo chat UI; SB-7 image API deferred by owner ruling (images stay ChatGPT-desktop then upload, which SB-2 delivers). Gates: shared build, vocab verifier PASS, server+shared typecheck clean, jest 2779/2779 (163 suites, 17 new). | support-book-sb6

- 2026-08-02 | Fix | POST /files/book now checks the book plane BEFORE touching Drive. Caught during a pre-promotion review of what SB-1..SB-6 actually exposes on a host where the book plane is unprovisioned — which prod is and will remain until it is deliberately provisioned. Every GraphQL resolver called assertBookPlane(); this REST route was the one surface that did not, so a caller holding book:illustrate or book:assemble (which the PRINCIPAL template does, D-#424) would have uploaded the bytes to Drive successfully, written a StoredFile row, and only THEN hung forever on registerAsset against an unopened mongoose connection — leaving an orphaned Drive file and a request that never answers. Now a 503 in Bangla before any Drive call. A static ordering test pins guard-before-upload, because the failure is invisible to a unit test that mocks the connection. The runbook §4 instruction to "restart and confirm the plane is live" could not have succeeded. The module was, as shipped, uncommissionable. Boot now attempts connectBookDb() and TOLERATES failure in both directions: a missing URI is a normal state logged as inert (a school without book production runs without it), and even a broken URI is logged rather than fatal — a book-plane problem must never stop attendance and homework from booting. - 2026-08-02 | Fix | Two defects found by the FIRST REAL end-to-end render on prod (D-#435). (a) The render worker opened only the book plane — its header even claimed that was deliberate — but filing a PDF writes StoredFile, which lives on the MAIN connection. Symptom was maximally misleading: both editions rendered, both font audits passed, BUILD OK logged, then the job died on storedfiles.insertOne() buffering timed out, which reads as Mongo being down and is actually a connection never opened. The worker now opens both; D-#404 is unaffected, since the boundary is that no BOOK model refs identity, not that a process may hold only one connection. (b) Chromium must not be the snap: snapd refuses to launch from inside a systemd SERVICE cgroup, and the trap is that a systemd-run probe PASSES because transient units are tolerated — so the check D-#433 demanded gives a false pass and only a persistent .service reproduces it. Switched to the non-snap Playwright arm64 build at /opt/chromium-pw, still reached via PUPPETEER_EXECUTABLE_PATH so the vendored renderer stays byte-identical. Runbook and code headers corrected. | support-book-sb4
- 2026-08-02 | Fix | Independently-produced images are no longer marked stale, and gate refusals reach the caller (D-#437). Found by pushing a real 10.5 MB C1-BAN image through prod. (a) chainStates marked a stage STALE whenever its upstream was absent — but absent has two meanings and only one is staleness: an upstream that EXISTED and vanished, versus one that never existed because the crop/upscale/strip chain ran on a laptop and only the finished COMPLIANT file was uploaded, which is exactly the workflow D-#409 blesses. Uploading a correctly-prepared image therefore marked it stale and the gate refused the book forever. Fixed using data already stored: inputFingerprint is null precisely when an artifact had no upstream at registration, so null means FRESH and set-but-unmatched means STALE; both directions pinned. (b) BuildGateError was registered for ALERTING but not for EXPOSURE, so Yoga masked the gate reasons as "Unexpected error" — telling the caller something broke when the system worked as designed and the reasons were the whole point. BuildGateError, ReviewRuleError, PatchShapeError and AuthorChatError are now exposed; the two registries answer different questions and a class needs both. | support-book-sb2

- 2026-08-03 | Feature | `myPermissions` — the caller's own effective permissions, as a root query riding the same round-trip as `me`. App navigation has always gated on the role TEMPLATE (`roleHasPermission`), which is blind to per-user grants. That was fine while every permission came from a role; it breaks for book production, where `book:*` sits only on the PRINCIPAL template and the illustrator/reviewer/assembler reach it by AC-1 grant (D-#405) — template gating would hide those screens from exactly the people they were built for. `useAuth().can(perm)` is now the preferred gate for new screens. No new authority and no new data surface: it reads the caller's own token, and every resolver still checks `callerHasPermission` for itself — hiding a tile is a courtesy, never the gate. Verified by executing a schema print: `myPermissions: [String!]` is on the built schema. | support-book-screens
- 2026-08-03 | Feature | The illustrator can upload finished artwork from the queue itself (SB-2). Reading the prompt was only half the loop; the file still had to be POSTed by hand. Each slot card now carries four one-tap upload buttons, one per chain stage, because the four stages ARE the workflow and a dropdown would add a tap to a screen someone visits two hundred times. Upload goes to the existing POST /files/book (png/jpeg ≤ 25 MB), which registers the lineage row — so the list REFETCHES after a successful upload rather than patching locally: an upload can make downstream stages stale (D-#417) and only the server knows the whole chain. generatorTool is stamped chatgpt-desktop on APPROVED rows (D-#419), the honest value until the API path lands. Verified: app typecheck + expo web export. NOT yet driven through a browser — click-through on the dev site before trusting it. | support-book-screens
- 2026-08-03 | Feature | The reviewer's, senior reviewer's and assembler's screens (SB-3/SB-4 app surfaces). Three screens close the loop the illustrator's queue opened. REVIEW: lessons expand in place with their round history, the README §7 checklist as chips, a verdict, and — inside the same card — the escalate form, because a reviewer who hits something they cannot rule on must be able to hand it up without abandoning the lesson (D-#410). Self-reviewed rounds are badged, which is the entire point of stamping them (D-#424). INBOX: threads oldest-first exactly as the server sorts them (the one waiting longest is blocking a lesson); the resolve form renders only for a caller holding book:review_senior, and says in words that resolving changes no lesson text — the author still patches it through the validator. ASSEMBLE: the gate and ALL its reasons are shown BEFORE the button, force is Principal-only and appears only after the gate has actually refused, render history keeps every job's PDFs downloadable, and book.json export sits here because the app must never become the only way to build a book (D-#406). Verified: app typecheck, expo web export (3865 modules), 2833 server tests, and every book GraphQL document validated against the real 8183-line schema. NOT yet driven through a browser. | support-book-screens

Owner report: the teacher re-uploaded the same paper four times on 2026-07-29. RETIRED rather than deleted, same reasoning as CT-C5-BAN-0001 on 2026-08-02: dashboards/summaries filter status PRINTED so CANCELLED removes them from the drill-down and the Overdue counts, while both rows carry a DELIVERED PrintRequest (real paper handed over) and the change stays reversible. CANCELLED rather than a row delete because the record stays fully intact for a one-flip restore, which is a stronger recovery guarantee than delete-plus-file; a JSON snapshot was written to server/scripts/backups/ as well. cancelClassTest could not do it (REQUESTED only, and no app UI), hence a pinned script: dry-run first, guarded on zero results and status PRINTED.
- 2026-08-03 | Fix | Support-book production moved out of the Admin hub into its own drawer group + stack. The four SB-2..SB-4 screens were cards inside the Admin tab, whose gate is the role TEMPLATE (content:import / user:manage) — a gate no granted illustrator or reviewer passes, so the screens were unreachable for them (book:* is per-user by D-#405). New SupportBookTab gated on effective book permissions, plus NavLeaf.perms so each leaf re-checks its own grant (an assembler no longer sees the reviewer queue). Verified in the running app: a TEACHER holding only book:read+book:illustrate now sees the group with exactly one leaf and opens it with no Admin tab present; the Principal sees all four; app tsc clean. | feat/support-book-tab
- 2026-08-03 | Fix | A Quran/Arabic leave cover now writes a RoutineSubstitution like a section cover does (owner report). The mirror write sat inside CoverService's section-only branch, so a subjectgroup cover recorded a StaffCoverSlot and nothing else — the class-note report kept naming the absent teacher AND publishClassNote refused the named proxy outright, since its cover gate reads RoutineSubstitution. Only the proxy GRANT is section-only. Prod backfill for 2026-08-03 onward created the 4 missing rows.
- 2026-08-03 | Fix | The Homework declare-pending report names the covering teacher rather than the absent one (owner report). Separate gap from the above: the substitution existed, ReconReportService just never read it and attributed from the routine slot's standing teacherId. Covers are now loaded per (slot, date) and win for that date.
- 2026-08-03 | Feature | Step one of book production finally has a screen: upload book.json or patch.json (SB-1). The module shipped with a complete server and every screen DOWNSTREAM of a book existing — the only way to put one in was a mutation typed into a terminal, which is why the owner's book picker was empty. New Book Production → বই আপলোড screen picks a .json (or takes a paste), works out structurally whether it is a full book.json or a §5 patch envelope (both carry book_id + lessons; only a patch carries patch_id), and hands it to the SAME `submitSupportBookPatch` the desktop path uses — one write path, no second implementation of "is this book valid" (D-#408). A full book.json is WRAPPED into an envelope, and the wrap is SHOWN before submitting rather than done invisibly: the patch id becomes the permanent name of that submission, `(bookId, patchId)` is uniquely indexed, and a repeat upload is refused on purpose — so naming it is the author's call, not a generated timestamp's. A RED is rendered as an OUTCOME, not an error: findings sorted REDs-first with their check, severity and location (পাঠ / block / slot), because an author whose patch bounced needs to read them rather than dismiss a banner. `policyMissing` is surfaced in warning colour — a patch validated against an incomplete policy set passed a weaker test than the author thinks (D-#403). Also a collapsed create-book form (book:manage) for a brand-new title. Gated book:author / book:manage per leaf. Verified: app typecheck, expo web export (3866 modules), 2836 server tests, 17/17 support-book GraphQL documents validated against the real schema. | support-book-import
- 2026-08-03 | Feature | Principal/Office can RETIRE a printed class test from the app, with a required reason (owner ask). Retire sets CANCELLED — the exam leaves every dashboard, report and Overdue count while the record survives — and is refused once any mark is entered. Restore puts it back, and a Retired-exams chip on the reports drill-down keeps a retired exam reachable, so retiring is never a one-way door. Replaces three hand-run prod scripts in two days: cancelClassTest only ever accepted REQUESTED, so a PRINTED exam had no route out at all.
- 2026-08-03 | Fix | The class-test results screen shows teachers "Submit" instead of "Publish results" (owner ask). The next screen only lets a teacher submit for approval; only an admin publishes, so the old label promised something the teacher could not do.
- 2026-08-03 | Feature | The reviewer can finally READ the lesson, and comment on it item by item (SB-3b, D-#440). Two gaps the owner found. (1) `SupportBookLesson` has stored `blocks` verbatim since SB-1 and nothing exposed them — `supportBookLessons` returned `blockCount`, a NUMBER — so the review screen asked for a verdict against a seven-item checklist over content nobody could see, which is the decorative-checklist failure the module's own design warns about. New `supportBookLessonContent(bookId, lessonNo)` returns blocks and image slots in reading order; single-lesson deliberately, because a 54-lesson book is 764 KB of JSON and shipping all of it to render one page is slow for everyone to serve nobody. Blocks come back PART-TYPED, PART-JSON on purpose: `id`/`layoutHint`/`textBn` are what a reviewer reads, and `json` carries the block verbatim — a typed mirror of every field would be a second contract to keep in step with a frozen renderer this repo does not own, and a new layout hint would need a schema change here. (2) Per-item comments: new `BookItemComment`, anchored to a block/slot/lesson via the SAME `ESCALATION_TARGETS` vocabulary (reused, not mirrored — one enum, two users). Neither a bigger feedback box (one paragraph for 493 blocks means the author re-reads to find each point) nor a lighter escalation (which means "I cannot rule on this, take it to the senior" and would flood the inbox or train reviewers to stop raising things). COMMENTS ARE RESOLVABLE AND AN UNRESOLVED ONE BLOCKS SIGN-OFF, mirroring the open-escalation rule: a note nobody has to answer is a note that gets skipped in a busy week, and resolved means the change is IN, not that the note was read. Resolving still edits NO lesson field — text moves only through a validated patch (D-#410). Either side may resolve: the author closes what they fixed, the reviewer withdraws what they thought better of. Verified: 2859/2859 server tests (16 new), app typecheck, expo web export (3867 modules), 21/21 support-book GraphQL documents validated against the real schema. | support-book-review-content
- 2026-08-03 | Chore | New `hasAnyPermission` auth scope, because an OR over the same scope is not expressible with `hasPermission` and Pothos's `$any` combines DIFFERENT scopes, one entry per name. The comment-resolve rule (either side of the channel may close a note) was first written as a `book:read` field gate with the real check inside the resolver — and the static resolver-gate guard caught it, correctly, as a mutation that reads as ungated. A permission check hidden in a resolver body is one the guard cannot see. The guard itself was TIGHTENED in the same change: it now reads the authScopes BLOCK only (a permission named in a description is not a gate) and requires EVERY permission in an OR to be a write, since one read-level member would let any production role through a mutation. | support-book-review-content
- 2026-08-03 | Fix | HOTFIX: the Class-test dashboard and Overdue-report chase were broken by the retire feature shipped an hour earlier. The new `retired` argument was added to the SHARED SUMMARY_ARG_DEFS/USE constants, but it exists only on classTestReportsStatus — so classTestPrincipalDashboard and classTestOverdueChase sent an argument they do not declare and failed the whole document with "Unknown argument retired". The arg now has its own defs and its own vars type, so the compiler prevents a repeat. Typecheck and the CI gate both passed the broken build: GraphQL documents are strings validated at runtime, which nothing in the gate checks against the server schema.
- 2026-08-03 | Feature | CI now validates every app GraphQL document against the real server schema (owner ask). Builds the schema via a new registerResolvers.ts (split out of index.ts so no express/Mongo boot is needed) and runs graphql-js validate over all 552 documents, catching unknown fields/arguments, wrong types and bad selection sets — the class of bug that broke the Class-test dashboard in prod today with every other check green. Module list is read from disk so new files are covered automatically. Found 7 pre-existing broken documents in finance.ts on its first run; those are quarantined behind an assertion that they are STILL broken, so fixing one forces its removal from the list.

Principal/Office or the exam's own teacher; refused once any result is entered, since the total is the denominator of every percentage and editing it would silently re-grade students. Previously there was no update path at all — only a script.

DUE is set by the overnight sweep — it means the deadline arrived, not that the work was missed — but it read "বাড়ির কাজ করে আনেনি" (did not do/bring the homework) to every parent in the class from midnight until the collection pass ran, 15 hours on the day it was reported. Now "বাড়ির কাজ জমা দেওয়ার সময় হয়েছে" in amber; the accusing wording and red stay with CHASE, which is a teacher's deliberate mark.
- 2026-08-03 | Fix | The subject filter on the homework and assignment workspaces is collapsible and CLOSED by default (owner ask). With six or seven subjects the chips wrapped to three rows and pushed the first card off screen. The collapsed header still names the active subject, so a filter can never silently hide work, and picking a subject closes the row again.
- 2026-08-03 | Fix | The script guard admits the en dash and em dash, and its comment no longer states a false reason (D-#442). The owner's first real patch upload red-failed on `—` in an image slot's scene_description and prompt. The rule's stated justification — that the four embedded Noto faces cannot render it, so it becomes a silent .notdef box in a printed book — was CHECKED AGAINST THE FONT FILES and is false: all four faces carry U+2013 and U+2014. I invented that rationale when porting the guard, and it mattered, because a rule defended on grounds that do not exist is a rule nobody can reason about or safely change. The real risk is narrower and still serious — a character the fonts genuinely lack (Arabic, CJK, emoji) renders as .notdef while the build SUCCEEDS, so the box reaches print silently — and that part of the allowlist is untouched. Both copies changed together (the app validator and the vendored CLI), since a string that passes one must pass the other. New `scriptGuard.test.ts` pins three things, the third of which is the point: what is admitted, what is still refused (Arabic honorifics especially, D-011), and that EVERY admitted codepoint is genuinely in the embedded faces — read from the TTF cmap, not asserted in a comment. It also executes the vendored CLI's own predicate and compares all 65,536 BMP codepoints, so the two guards cannot drift silently. 2893/2893 server tests. | support-book-policy-screen
- 2026-08-03 | Feature | Governance documents can be loaded from the app (SB-1, D-#403). The first real upload came back with all eight documents under "Policy documents not found" and a RED reading `letter inventory missing — a C1–C2 বাংলা book cannot merge unaudited` — not a content error, but the validator refusing because it cannot run the audit, and refusing rather than skipping so an unaudited primer never merges looking like it passed. Loading a document was terminal-only, which made the whole upload path unusable by the people it is for. New Book Production → নীতি-নথি screen lists all eight keys with their active version or a "missing" badge, shows the set hash every patch is stamped with, and takes a file per key. THE PER-BOOK / PROGRAMME-WIDE SPLIT IS HANDLED FOR YOU: `LETTER_INVENTORY` belongs to one book and the other seven are programme-wide — send a book id with a programme-wide doc and it becomes invisible to every other book; omit it on the inventory and the audit never finds it. The screen sends the right one per key and labels which is which, rather than asking anyone to remember. Activating supersedes the prior version and never deletes it, so there is no delete action. Gated book:manage. Verified: app typecheck, expo web export (3868 modules), 2893 server tests, 23/23 support-book GraphQL documents validated against the real schema. | support-book-policy-screen

NOTE: permissions ride the JWT, so anyone whose access changed must log out and back in.

Adds retireHolidayException, which flips active:false rather than deleting; all seven read sites already filter active:true, so one flag withdraws the holiday everywhere while the record survives.

Proven to bite by sabotaging both invariants and watching them fail. NOT an end-to-end check — the app workspace has no test runner, so “typing survives a reload” still needs a human on the dev site.

Guardian reminders ride the existing D-#260 per-day dedupe. New Reports-hub screen সাপ্তাহিক বাড়ির কাজ জমা-বাকি রিপোর্ট (homeworkWeeklyUnsubmitted, Principal/Office by role) with the guardian-identical message behind a manual wa.me button per student — the bridge to contact-only families — plus print/CSV.
