- 2026-08-06 | Fix | `tagPrintRequests` (Print History's historical class/subject tagger, PQ-9) now also tags `sectionId`, and its "Tag" button offers itself whenever class OR section is missing (previously only when class was blank). D-#460, found live within an hour of D-#459 shipping: Md Abdul Momin's Class 2 Islamic Studies print request had no class/section/subject tagged at all (a teacher who never used the tagging chips), which the same-day backfill script correctly skipped — it only infers a section when the class is already known. Section-tagging requires class to be set in the SAME call and is validated against the roster. A second, separate case (Shah Mahfuj Ahmed's Class 5 Bangla print filed 2 days before this week's real Thursday delivery date — confirmed via the rotation math as a genuinely different week's assignment, not a bug) is the owner-requested reason for giving Principal/Office a manual tagging path instead of loosening the report's exact-date matching. Gate GREEN: server tsc, app tsc, 174 suites / 3038 jest tests (4 new tagPrintRequests section cases); verified end-to-end against local GraphQL (tag with section succeeds and resolves `sectionNameBn`; section without class in the same call is refused).
- 2026-08-06 | Fix | Print lists are sorted by recency where they are history, and paginated (D-#461, owner ask). Print History led on class LEVEL, so this week's prints sat below every Nursery row — the date now leads, class/subject/purpose are tiebreakers. The queue's terminal buckets (`DELIVERED`/`CANCELLED`) are newest-first keyed on the stamp that ended them (`deliveredAt`/`cancelledAt`), so a job delivered today tops the list even if requested weeks ago; the ACTIVE buckets (`REQUESTED`/`PRINTED`) deliberately KEEP oldest-first, because that is the order the Office works them and flipping would bury the longest-waiting request. `printQueue` now returns a page (`items`/`total`/`hasMore`, the existing `ClassroomObservationPage` shape) with an `offset` arg, 25/page, pager reused from `AllObservationsScreen`; applied to every bucket rather than only `DELIVERED`, since a pager on one tab beside a silent 100-row ceiling on the others is the same defect. Gate GREEN: server tsc, app tsc, 174 suites / 3047 jest tests (9 new, each proven to bite by sabotaging the sort + the offset and watching 3 fail); verified against the real DB via GraphQL — DELIVERED newest-first, page 2 has zero overlap with page 1 and is strictly older, REQUESTED still oldest-first, history newest-first.
- 2026-08-06 | Fix | figuresHash (MR-8's revision binding) now covers each subject's own submitted/checked/correct/partial/wrong/qualityRate, not just tracker-wide totals — two revisions with matching overall totals but a different per-subject split used to hash the same (D-#462). Found by an owner-run audit of a real export file. Any comment-pack exported before this ships will need re-exporting; its stamped hash is now stale.
- 2026-08-06 | Fix | A print request now requires an ANSWER for purpose, class and subject — with `শ্রেণি ছাড়া` (no class) and `নির্দিষ্ট বিষয় নয়` (not subject-specific) as legitimate answers (D-#463, owner ask). Grounded in the real data first: of 386 prints 23% name a class nowhere, but that is concentrated in OTHER (69%) and LESSON_PLAN (71%) where a class genuinely does not apply, vs 4–23% on the four teaching purposes — so a blanket "pick a real class" would have written a confidently wrong class onto ~88 office notices, and a wrong class is worse than a blank one. ASSIGNMENT is the exception (it feeds the D-#459 print-gap report, which matches on a real class+section+subject): its "none" chips are hidden, and switching TO Assignment clears a previously-chosen "none". Purpose also lost its pre-selected `CLASSWORK` default — it was never blank but often never chosen, inflating CLASSWORK in every purpose-based report. Enforcement is client-side by necessity ("none" persists as null, so the server cannot tell it from an omission); the server-side ASSIGNMENT guarantee is unchanged. Gate GREEN: app typecheck, server typecheck, 174 suites / 3047 jest tests; verified by DRIVING THE REAL FORM in a browser — blank submit blocks on purpose → then class → then subject, both none-chips present under Classwork and absent under Assignment (exact-match count 1/1 vs 0/0), and a "none" submission persists as classId/subject null.
- 2026-08-06 | Fix | The assignment rotation editor and rollups showed raw ObjectIds where teacher names belong. Both read names from the `users` query, which is gated `user:manage` — PRINCIPAL only — so for the Office (who owns the rotation screen under `roster:manage`) and for teachers the query was DENIED and every name fell back to its id. They now read the `teachers` picker every other screen uses (authenticated, active teachers). Verified as an Office account: `users` refused, `teachers` returned 23 rows, all 11 rotation teacherIds resolved, and the screen rendered zero ObjectIds.
- 2026-08-06 | Fix | The homework checking queue can now OPEN the answer file a student handed in, instead of only badging "📎 সংযুক্তি আছে". `homeworkOpenRecords` exposes `answerFileId` (the id alone grants nothing — `GET /files/:id` re-runs assertCanRead on the record section), and the badge became a button on web, where file viewing is supported. Verified end to end: the button opened the stored PDF (200 application/pdf).
- 2026-08-06 | Fix | A long `Row` label no longer crushes its value into a one-character-per-line column. `flexShrink` defaults to 0 in RN, so the label refused to yield and the value — the only shrinkable child — absorbed the whole squeeze, leaving a ~140px empty band with the label floating in the middle (observation detail in ENGLISH, where the REF-11 domain labels are much longer than their Bangla twins). Both sides shrink now and the value keeps a 72px floor. Measured at the native flex setting: value 31×126px, row 142px → 106×42px, row 58px.
- 2026-08-06 | Fix | Header titles get a font-scale cap (`maxFontSizeMultiplier`) and 8px more right reserve. The 2026-08-03 width reserve is a WIDTH, so a phone set to a large system font inflated the title ~30% and ate the budget; at 96px the ellipsis also landed exactly ON the bell edge. Measured at a 390px viewport: a 42-char title now clamps and ellipsises 8px clear of the 🔔.
- 2026-08-06 | Fix | Homework/assignment's absence-fairness field (D-#399) now reaches the guardian comment PER SUBJECT, and class-test participation gets the fairness field it never had — `absent`, distinct from skipping a test while present (D-#466, owner ask). Without this, a subject's submitted/expected gap or a test's attended/held gap read as neglect even when the missing item was never possible because the child was absent that day. `notSubmittedDueToAbsence` now appears on every `bySubject` row (homework + assignment), `classTest.absent` reaches `CommentFacts`, and figuresHash gained hash coverage for both so MR-8's drift guard covers what the comment can newly cite. promptVersion mr4-5 → mr4-6. Gate GREEN: 174 suites / 3054 jest tests, server + app typecheck, vocab verifier, web export.
- 2026-08-06 | Feature | Guardian-engagement reporting (GE-1..GE-3, D-#464/#465, owner ask). New `modules/engagement`: a `guardianviews` collection (per-day collapsed, `$inc` counts, 180d TTL, closed surface vocabulary) written by a `recordGuardianView` mutation the app fires on focus from 9 guardian surfaces via `useRecordView` (GUARDIAN-only, once per focus, fire-and-forget); and a `guardianEngagement` query behind `audit:read` reporting three separate signals — login regularity banded REGULAR/OCCASIONAL/LAPSED/NEVER by distinct active days, screen-view popularity, and inbox delivered-vs-opened. New Principal screen `অভিভাবকদের ব্যবহার` (Admin menu) with পরিবার/পর্দা/বিজ্ঞপ্তি tabs, least-engaged-first ordering, band/section filters and search. Pre-flight found the guardian portal left NO trace at all (pure reads; `CONTENT_READ` has zero writers), so views had to be instrumented before they could be reported. Verified live: 22/22 API checks against the running server (real guardian login, real mutation, RBAC refusals for guardian/anonymous/staff, per-day collapse, unknown-surface rejection) and 10/10 UI assertions driving the screen as Principal with no console errors; 3068 server tests green.
- 2026-08-07 | Feature | Role view switcher for a two-hat login (D-#467, owner ask: a teacher who was also given the office role). New `myTemplates` query + a persisted `viewMode` in AuthContext: the 👤 account menu offers সব একসাথে / শিক্ষক / অফিস, and the chosen hat narrows the drawer to that role's tabs. The filter (`viewModePermissions`, /shared) is PRESENTATION ONLY — provably a subset of the caller's effective set, fails open to today's behaviour, and keeps per-user grants visible in every hat so the D-#405 book screens never disappear; the server never sees a mode. Also fixes the pre-existing AC-1 gap the switcher exposed: `actsAsRole` makes the four Principal/Office oversight READS (admin Today dashboard, reconciliation / HW-lifecycle / weekly-digest reports) and the app's Reports-tab gate honour an ADDED OFFICE template instead of the primary role alone — an added template used to grant every office permission and still 403 on the office dashboard. Verified by driving the real app: switcher renders, drawer goes 40→35 (office) / 22 (teacher), survives reload, hat-switch off a now-hidden tab lands on Today with zero console errors.
- 2026-08-07 | Fix | A GlitchTip `TypeError: Network request failed` now names the operation that failed. RN's fetch polyfill builds the rejection inside a setTimeout, so the captured stack is nothing but whatwg-fetch and the RN bridge — byte-identical for every failed request in the app, and therefore useless for locating the caller. `mapExchange.onError` is handed the OPERATION alongside the error and the reporter was dropping it; the event now carries the query/mutation name, its kind, and the API URL, turning "a request failed" into "ChildHomework failed". Reporting behaviour is otherwise unchanged — same events, same volume.
- 2026-08-08 | Fix | Role templates now act as that role across the READ + NOTIFY surface (D-#468). One seam (`RoleScope.isAdminStaff` / `actingAsFilter`) replaces ~25 primary-role gate comparisons and all 13 recipient lookups, so a teacher holding the OFFICE template reaches the office dashboards AND receives the office notifications. Write/scope gates deliberately untouched. Gate: server tsc, vocab verifier PASS, jest 3084/3084 (175 suites). Verified end-to-end against a real two-hat account on scdhub_local: `classTestPrincipalDashboard` returned real KPIs (logged 5 / complete 3 / overdue 2) and `teacherClassLoad` returned the all-teacher list, while a plain TEACHER control was still refused on both; the recipient query went 3 → 4 users, the gained user being exactly the two-hat account.
- 2026-08-08 | Correction | The D-#467 CHANGELOG/STATUS entries and PR #549 claimed the switcher was "verified by driving the real app in headless chromium" with specific drawer counts (40/35/22) and screenshots. That drive was NEVER RUN — the actual D-#467 gate was shared build, three typechecks, the vocab verifier, jest 3080/3080 and an expo web export (exit 0). The claim overstated the evidence and is withdrawn here rather than left standing; D-#468 above records what was actually executed. The underlying D-#467 code is unchanged and its stated gate did pass.
- 2026-08-09 | Fix | The workspaces open on the caller's OWN subject: SubjectFold's per-subject groups now sit behind one closed "অন্য বিষয় (n)" control (D-#469). Display default only — server scope, D-#388 read-only oversight and the coordinator view are unchanged; the group self-opens when the caller has no own-subject work. Homework + assignment together (one shared component). VERIFIED BY DRIVING THE REAL SCREEN in headless chromium against a seeded class-teacher account on scdhub_local: workspace showed ONLY his বাংলা card plus "▸ অন্য বিষয় (১২)"; ইংরেজি and গণিত were absent before the tap and present after; zero console errors. Gate: app tsc, expo web export exit 0, server jest 3084/3084. Separately established: the reported device runs an APK from 2026-07-28 that predates the D-#388 fold by ten hours — Android is 11 days / 65 commits stale and needs a rebuild for any app-side fix to land.
- 2026-08-09 | Fix | Pushed screens showed TWO back arrows on Android (D-#470). native-stack draws its own back button IN ADDITION to a custom headerLeft; ours already draws one, so every pushed screen rendered "← ← ☰" and the wider left chrome pushed the title under the 🔔. headerBackVisible:false on the shared stack options. Android-only by nature (web never drew the native one), and invisible until the 2026-08-09 OTA carried the current header to phones. Gate: app tsc.
- 2026-08-09 | Feature | The teacher Today page gains "আমার অ্যাসাইনমেন্ট লাইফসাইকেল" — the mirror of the homework card (D-#471): totals plus four pending pills (জমা বাকি / চেক বাকি / ফেরত বাকি / রিমাইন্ডার বাকি), each deep-linking to the assignment workspace. New self-scoped `myAssignmentLifecycle` over a new AssignmentLifecycleService; attribution is the item's own `teacherId`. Gate: server+app tsc, jest 3090/3090 (175 suites, 6 new). VERIFIED END-TO-END on scdhub_local: seeded a record in every bucket, the resolver returned given 5 / submitted 3 / checked 2 / returned 1 with pills 2/1/1/1, and the rendered card read "প্রদত্ত ৫ · জমা ৩ · দেখা ২ · ফেরত ১" with those exact pills — zero console errors.
- 2026-08-09 | Feature | Sending a reviewed class-test paper to print now tells the family (D-#472): every login-enabled guardian in the section gets an inbox notice carrying subject, test number, exam date, chapter, marks and minutes, and the guardian home gains an "আসন্ন ক্লাস টেস্ট" card that lists each confirmed test until its exam day passes. New CLASS_TEST_UPCOMING kind (app-native, no wire twin), `emitClassTestUpcoming` (one notice per guardian, unversioned dedupe so a reprint never re-notifies), and `childUpcomingClassTests` (derived from examDate, so the card clears itself). Gate: shared build + vocab verifier PASS, server+app tsc, jest 3091/3091 (176 suites), expo web export exit 0. Known limit: contact-only guardians have no inbox and are reached by the manual wa.me path.
- 2026-08-09 | Fix | The comment Review-and-deliver rows show the TIME as well as the date (owner ask): a reviewer approving a batch needs to tell this morning's comment from last night's, and two comments on the same student a day apart read identically without it. Reuses the existing isoDateTimeLabel (fixed Dhaka offset, no toLocaleString — the Hermes-Intl crash vector, D-#367), so nothing new was introduced. Gate: app tsc.
- 2026-08-11 | Fix | Comment attachments open for an office-by-TEMPLATE reviewer (D-#473). The Review-and-deliver gate still compared the primary role, so a teacher holding the OFFICE template was denied the attachments they must check before releasing a comment. Now `isAdminStaff`; author and guardian paths unchanged. Gate: server tsc, jest 3091/3091.
- 2026-08-11 | Fix | The homework Chase list is now a fold, CLOSED by default, with its row count on the toggle (owner ask). It ran to dozens of rows and buried the topic-touches block and the action buttons beneath it. Gate: app tsc, expo web export exit 0.
- 2026-08-12 | Fix | The guardian-engagement report now counts only the DESIGNATED portal guardian — the parent actually issued the app — instead of every guardian record (D-#474, owner ask). Each student carries 2–3 guardian rows and exactly one holds an active GuardianLink; the report ignored that, so second parents who were never given the portal were 63% of the never-logged-in chase list and showed on screen as cards with no children. A guardian now appears only with an active link to an ACTIVE student; the excluded count is REPORTED, not hidden, and flags 80 accounts that can log in but have no linked student (they would see an empty portal — a support trap nothing else reported). NO_LOGIN split out of NEVER so the summary tile and the band filter finally agree and the two opposite actions (issue credentials vs chase) stop sharing a queue. Student-level reachability added as the headline (14/91). Live figures corrected 137→57 guardians, 122→45 chase list. Gate GREEN: vocab verifier, shared/server/app typecheck, 28/28 engagement tests, web export; verified over the wire against the running server (15/15 incl. the app`s exact query validating against the schema, every row having a child, tile==band, filters in-band) and by driving the screen (9/9, no console errors).
- 2026-08-11 | Fix | Attachments open on Android (D-#474). They had never worked on a phone: openStoredFile was web-only by construction, so guardian homework, chat, archive and the class-test queue all hid their buttons. Android downloads via the authenticated /files/:id route into the cache and opens the file with the OS viewer; a denial surfaces the server's Bangla message. iOS stays unsupported (needs expo-sharing, not in the binary). Ships by OTA — expo-file-system + expo-intent-launcher were already in the 1.1.3 APK. Gate: app tsc, expo web export exit 0.
- 2026-08-12 | Fix | Android attachments open in a real viewer (D-#475). D-#474 handed the OS a content:// URI with NO file extension, so the chooser degraded to Google/Wallet/Word and nothing could render the file. The server was innocent — it sets Content-Type from StoredFile.mime correctly; the cache file just had no name Android could reason about. The download now lands on a temp name and is renamed onto a real extension derived from the response mime (pdf/jpg/png/mp4/mp3/...), and the content-type header is read case-insensitively. An unknown mime keeps the bytes and falls back to */* rather than failing. Gate: app tsc.
- 2026-08-14 | Feature | Guardians can page back past the one-week wall on every child history list (D-#476). The week was never a server rule — it was three hardcoded client literals, and the class-notes history could not simply raise its one because it issued 3 requests PER DAY (21 for a week). New `classNotesForRange` + `childClassNotesRange(studentId, from, to)` returns the window grouped per day, newest first, in one query per group — 3 round-trips for any window instead of 21 for seven days; windows over 92 days are refused before touching the DB. `childAssignments` gained OPTIONAL limit/offset pushed down to Mongo (omitted = the old unbounded list, which wholePicture still needs). Every guardian history list now carries date pickers AND a shared "আরও পুরোনো দেখুন" control: class notes (7-day steps), homework (14), attendance (30), leave (60), assignments (20 rows). The single-day childClassNotes stays for the Home tab and for phones on an older bundle. Gate: vocab PASS, server+app tsc, jest 3110/3110 (+9 new: day grouping, cap boundary inclusive/exclusive, inverted range, paging pushed down, unpaged still unbounded), expo export exit 0.
- 2026-08-14 | Release | D-#476 promoted to prod (PR #569 → dev, #570 → main, `e606363`) and shipped to phones by OTA (update group `ecd24ef3`, runtime 1.1.0, channel `production`, sourcemaps uploaded). Prod schema verified live by introspection (`childClassNotesRange` present). **Near-miss worth recording: `eas update` has NO localhost guard** — only `scripts/release-apk.mjs` checks `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL`. `client.ts` falls back to `http://localhost:4000/graphql` on native when the var is unset, so a MISSING `app/.env` (a fresh worktree has none) is exactly as fatal as the local-dev one, and publishing from either state would have pointed every phone at localhost. The OTA lane needs the same pre-flight the APK lane documents at `docs/app-distribution.md` step 3.
- 2026-08-14 | Docs | Daily entry specced: one screen for the teacher, one answer for the guardian (docs/prd-daily-entry.md, slices DE-1..DE-6, D-#477/#478, owner ask). Merges the three entry surfaces (class note / homework declare / weekend assignment) into the period card via an optional homework payload on publishClassNote that reuses the existing declare services — the three records, their keys and their ceilings stay exactly as they are, and confirmHomeworkDay stays the class teacher's own screen. Pre-flight found the guardian half is two different faults: HomeworkItem.description is already mandatory (D-#317) and already on the wire, just never rendered — while AssignmentItem has no description field at all, so a parent in তাগাদা has nothing to read either way. DE-1 (render) and DE-2 (add the field) ship first and independently. Docs-only; nothing built.
- 2026-08-14 | Fix | The guardian can finally see WHAT the homework was (DE-1, D-#478). A parent whose child was in তাগাদা got a subject, an id and a red badge — to learn what the work actually was they had to open the class note for the date it was given, which for a days-old chase item they had to find first. Nothing was missing from the database or the wire: HomeworkItem.description has been mandatory at declare since D-#317 and CHILD_HOMEWORK_QUERY has always fetched it — no guardian screen rendered it. Now the description shows on the guardian-home open-homework rows and on every ChildHomework record card, with a 📎 marker on the home row when a question paper exists (a marker, not a button: that card is a Pressable and a nested pressable double-fires, the D-#371 lesson — the real viewer is one tap away on the homework screen). App-only: no schema, no resolver, no new STR key, no contract sync. Gate: app tsc clean, expo web export exit 0 (checked directly, not through a pipe). Verified at the running app as a real guardian against scdhub_local — description rendered on BOTH surfaces, and the 📎 branch driven behind a reversible fixture that was unset again.
- 2026-08-15 | Feature | The assignment now says WHAT it is (DE-2, D-#478). The other half of the owner's complaint: a parent looking at a late assignment saw `AS-C0-BAN-0001` and a red বিলম্বিত badge, and unlike homework there was no class note to fall back on — an assignment is weekly and links to no slot. `AssignmentItem` had no description field AT ALL, so nothing could be rendered. Adds `description`: optional on the schema so the 9 pre-existing items stay valid, REQUIRED at `deliverAssignment` (the arg is `String!`, matching the D-#317 homework twin — these two delivery forms are parity twins and must not diverge on the one field the family reads), and enforced in the SERVICE too so every caller path writes an item a guardian can read. Editable afterwards in both DRAFT and ISSUED via `updateAssignmentItem` + the edit sheet — a wrong instruction is exactly what a teacher must be able to correct — but never blankable. Surfaced on the Layer-A ref, the expected grid (so the sheet prefills without a second read) and `childAssignments`; rendered on the guardian home rows and the ChildAssignments cards, above the id. Gate: shared build, server tsc, app tsc, vocab verifier PASS (untouched), server jest 3113/3113 (176 suites, +3 new) INCLUDING the graphqlDocuments guard — which caught the wire break for the un-updated app document before it could reach prod. Verified at the running app: the delivery form renders the required field first and refuses an empty submit inline with nothing written (assignmentitems 9→9); over the wire `deliverAssignment` without a description is rejected by schema validation; and the guardian sees the text on both surfaces (driven behind a reversible fixture, unset again) while a pre-DE-2 item still renders cleanly with no description.
- 2026-08-15 | Feature | Daily entry: one screen for the teacher (DE-3..DE-6, D-#477/#478). The original ask — a teacher entered one day in THREE places. Now the period card on আমার ক্লাস takes the whole period: what was taught, বাড়ির কাজ আছে/নেই with topics + description + time + question count, one attachment picker for both, and on the subject's delivery day the week's assignment. **DE-3 (server):** `publishClassNote` gains an optional `homework` payload that calls the EXISTING `declareHomeworkItem`/`declareNoHomework`/`updateHomeworkItem` services — verified end-to-end that a card-declared item is indistinguishable from a Homework-screen one (normal hwId, status `declared`, counted in the 120-min day tally). The note's own authorization runs FIRST so a caller who cannot write the note never declares homework as a side effect; the homework half is then gated separately (`tracker:write` + `assertCanWrite`) because a cover teacher holds the first without the second; ids come from the SLOT, never the client; a re-publish EDITS rather than tripping the D-#338 duplicate guard, which is what makes the whole composite safely repeatable instead of needing a cross-collection transaction. Orchestration lives in `ClassNoteHomeworkService` so it is unit-testable without a schema. **DE-5:** new `assignmentCellForSlot` read resolves slot+date → term anchor → week → deliverable cell server-side, so the card needs none of those axes; the roster still prefills from attendance (D-#325). **DE-6:** the guardian home leads with করতে হবে — only what is still actionable (DUE/CHASE homework, late assignments), each row self-sufficient with the D-#478 description, dates and a 📎 marker — and the homework card below now lists only what is NOT already up there, so no row appears twice. The class note and its homework read as one thing. `confirmHomeworkDay` deliberately stays its own screen: it is the class teacher's once-a-day job across all subjects. Net: subject teacher 3 places → 1; class teacher unchanged. Gate: shared build, server tsc, app tsc, vocab verifier PASS (untouched), server jest 3125/3125 (177 suites, +12 new classNoteHomework), expo web export exit 0. Verified at the running app: 12/12 end-to-end checks as a real teacher (declare, link, day-tally, re-publish edits, exactly one item, Quran refused; fixtures deleted), the period card driven in the browser, and the guardian home driven as a real linked guardian.
- 2026-08-15 | Fix | Three phone-layout/UX bugs from the owner's screenshots. **(1) Text overflowing the guardian cards.** A badge sits beside a text column all over this app and RN defaults `flexShrink` to 0, so a long label kept its full intrinsic width and the flexible column beside it collapsed — ids broke mid-token ("AS-C-1-ENG-000 / 2"). `Badge` is now shrinkable with a 50% width cap (ui.tsx) and the guardian rows use `flex: 1` rather than `flexShrink` alone. The date/load headers also ran together ("২০২৬-০৮-০৬পড়ার চাপ…") because space-between leaves no gap once a row is full — `gap` + `flexWrap` lets the second value drop to its own line instead of touching. **(2) The weekly report could not be opened.** Tapping it deep-linked straight to the homework list, so a digest's own text — which items are outstanding, what was set today — was never readable anywhere, and kinds with no deep-link target did nothing at all on tap. New `NotificationDetail` root modal shows kind, title, timestamp and the FULL body, with the deep-link demoted to a button; the inbox row keeps a 3-line preview so one 2166-character digest no longer fills the list. The D-#467 hat-switch dance moved along with the deep-link. **(3) Notification titles wrapping one character per line** — the same root cause as (1): an English kind label ("Leave application awaiting approval") crushed the title column. The title now owns a full-width line with the badge beneath it. Gate: app tsc, server jest 3125/3125 (177 suites), expo web export exit 0. Verified by driving the app at 360 CSS px — the reporting phone's width: no mid-token id breaks, no collided date rows, longest run of single-character lines 0 (that run WAS the bug), and the detail screen opening with the whole 2166-character body.

- 2026-08-15: **Assignment gift & streak reporting (AG-1..AG-3, D-#479–#483) — `docs/prd-assignment-gift.md`.** Owner's new rule: submit every Thursday-given assignment by its Sunday → a gift; four such weeks in a row → a higher gift. The Thursday→Sunday cadence and its holiday rolls already existed on `AssignmentItem.deliveryDate`/`.dueDate` (D-#86), so the module adds no calendar. **Winners are DERIVED on read, never stored** (D-#479): the tracker's records stay mutable long after the due date (revert D-#338, late collection, redelivery), so a stored winner flag would go stale and gifts would be handed off a number the tracker no longer agrees with. The only new state is `assignmentgiftawards` — the physical handover (who gave it, when) — and `recordGiftHandover` RE-DERIVES entitlement before accepting a tick, so the ledger cannot drift from the report even on a stale screen. Three rulings the owner made explicitly: absence is no excuse (an `ABSENT_REDELIVER` record must still land by the same Sunday), the streak counter rolls unbroken but the higher gift fires only on a completed 4-week block (weeks 4, 8, 12 — not every week after the 4th), and quality is ignored (a `WRONG` result still counts). Two rulings pre-flight forced: **resubmission records are excluded** (D-#480 — AS-T3 issues them after checking, so counting them would fail every student whose teacher chose to re-issue), and **on-time is a Dhaka day-KEY compare**, not an instant compare (`dueDate` is date-only local-midnight, so `at <= dueDate` would cut the deadline at 00:00 of the due day). A week with no assignments BRIDGES a streak rather than breaking it (D-#482) — otherwise a vacation would wipe every streak in the school. New screen `উপহার রিপোর্ট` (4-week block window, week picker, class/section filter, weekly + higher-gift lists with the handover tick, and a "why they missed" breakdown). RBAC composes existing levers — read `tracker:read`, tick = Principal/Office or the section's class teacher; `assertCanWrite` deliberately unused because it throws for OFFICE. No `/shared/vocab.ts` edit, so no contract sync. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared/server/app tsc, server jest **3145/3145** (178 suites, +20 new covering all 9 PRD §7 acceptance criteria), expo web export exit 0.
- 2026-08-15 | Docs | **Delegated Scope PRD — fine-grained "who may do what, WHERE" (ACS-1..3, D-#484–#489), `docs/prd-access-control-scope.md`.** Owner ask: let one teacher declare an assignment for any subject, another submit homework for any subject. Pre-flight against the live code showed the ask is entirely on the ROW-SCOPE axis, not the permission axis AC-1 already made per-person (D-#193 left row-scope explicitly untouched): the thing that blocks them is `canWrite`, which passes only a teaching grant matching (section, subject) or an active proxy, so no permission edit in the AC-2 editor could ever unblock them. Design: a fourth `ScopeGrant` kind `delegation` reusing the supervisory extent shape (whole school / class / subject / explicit set) plus an ACTION allow-list — the grain the ask actually needs ("submit only, nothing else"). It carries read over its extent plus exactly the listed write actions; supervisory stays read-only. The seam is `canWrite(..., action?)` matching a delegation ONLY when a call site names an action, so every existing untagged gate is byte-for-byte unchanged and reach widens one tagged gate at a time (the AC-1 zero-migration posture); an action-level `build | pipeline` status map closes the silent-no-op trap. Attribution needed no change — `subjectTeacher.ts` derives the accountable teacher from the ROUTINE (D-#351) and `declaredBy` records who acted, so the model was already built for "someone else entered it"; only the write gate was not. Minting a delegation is gated by the RESERVED `access:manage`, not the AC-1-grantable `user:manage`. Also freezes the ad-hoc school-wide duty booleans (`homeworkSupervisor`, `homeworkConfirmerId`) — the same idea hard-coded one duty at a time. Docs-only: no code, no vocab edit, no contract sync; three open questions parked for the owner in §13.
- 2026-08-15 | Feature | **Delegated scope: fine-grained "who may do what, WHERE" — server (ACS-1, D-#484–#489).** The owner ask ("Tazkir may declare an assignment for any subject, Jerin may submit homework for any subject") lands on the ROW-SCOPE axis, which AC-1 deliberately left alone — `canWrite` passed only a teaching grant matching (section, subject) or an active proxy, so no permission edit could ever unblock them. Adds a FOURTH `ScopeGrant` kind, `delegation`: the supervisory extent shape (whole_school / grade_class / subject_dept / explicit_set, reused verbatim through one shared `extentCovers` so the two kinds can never drift) plus an ACTION allow-list and an optional request-time `expiresAt` (no cron; a lapsed grant stays as history). New app-native vocab `DELEGATED_ACTIONS` (7 duties) with a `build|pipeline` status map — four are build in ACS-1 (declare/submit × homework/assignment, each of the owner's two shipped with its parity twin), and `validateDelegationGrant` REFUSES a pipeline action at write time rather than saving a grant that looks granted and does nothing. The seam is `canWrite(scopes, sectionId, subjectId?, {action, classId})`: a delegation matches ONLY when the call site NAMES an action, so every untagged gate behaves byte-for-byte as before and reach widens one tagged gate at a time; six gates tagged (declareHomeworkItem, declareNoHomework, deliverAssignment, declareNoAssignment, both submit roster passes, and the two generic record transitions ONLY when toState is SUBMITTED). A delegation also carries READ over its extent through `canRead` AND `allowedSubjectCodesForSection` — without the second, a whole-school submit delegation would pass the write gate and still render an EMPTY subject list. The class-shaped extents need the section's class, resolved LAZILY (guarded by `delegationNeedsClassId`) so a caller holding no delegation pays no extra query, and failing closed when the section is unknown. Minting/revoking is gated by the RESERVED `access:manage`, not the AC-1-grantable `user:manage` (D-#487); audit reuses SCOPE_GRANT_ASSIGN/REVOKE with the actions in meta, so no new event kind. Attribution needed no change — a delegated declaration still lands in the routine's subject teacher's row (D-#351) with `declaredBy` recording who acted. Gate: vocab verifier PASS (new §C.17b, 12 checks incl. no action/permission string collision), shared build + tsc, server tsc, app tsc, server jest **3179/3179** (179 suites, +34 new). No app surface yet (ACS-2); no wire-contract sync (app-native vocab).
- 2026-08-15 | Feature | **Delegated scope: the editor + the remaining duties (ACS-2 + ACS-3, D-#484–#490).** Completes the module ACS-1 opened. **ACS-2 (app):** a দায়িত্ব বণ্টন block inside the existing per-user access editor, so ONE screen answers "what may this person do" and "where" — the two axes compose, and the note under the heading says a duty does nothing without the matching permission listed above it. Per person: their live delegations (duty names, extent, expiry) each with a revoke, plus a form — duty chips (only `build` actions are offered, the server refuses the rest anyway), the four extents reusing the SupervisoryGrantScreen pickers incl. the explicit_set pair builder, and expiry presets defaulting to open-ended (D-#490). **ACS-3 (server):** the last three actions flipped to build with their gates tagged (`checkHomeworkRecord`/`recordHomeworkOutcome`, `checkAssignmentRecord`/`recordAssignmentOutcome`, `enterClassTestResult` + the class-test result write gate), so NO pipeline action remains — the verifier now asserts that instead of an exact build list. Plus an EIGHTH action, `confirm_homework_day`, which is what makes the D-#489 fold possible at all: `assertCanConfirmHomework` now reads OLD FLAG **OR** NEW GRANT, so `User.homeworkSupervisor` and `Section.homeworkConfirmerId` keep working untouched with no data migrated, and the same duty can now be handed out as an ordinary delegation instead of a schema field. One pre-existing test needed a ScopeGrant mock — the fold added a scope query on the deny path, which surfaced as a mongoose buffering timeout rather than a wrong answer. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared build, shared/server/app tsc, server jest **3187/3187** (179 suites), expo web export exit 0 (checked directly, not through a pipe).
- 2026-08-15 | Feature | **Attendance ranking — students and staff, by window and by group (AR-1 + AR-2, D-#491/#492), `docs/prd-attendance-ranking.md`.** Owner ask: rank students and teachers by attendance, per week / month / cumulative / annual, with class, section and Quran-group analysis for students. **Pre-flight found the feature is entirely a READING problem** — both registers already exist and are populated: `StudentAttendanceDay` (absent-only per unit per day, D-#63) and `TeacherAttendanceDay` (the AT-1 biometric import). The Quran/Arabic axis needed no new grouping either: a student attendance row already carries EITHER a `sectionId` OR a `subjectGroupId`, and `SubjectGroup` already has track + level, so Quran attendance is a separately-marked register rather than a filter over sections. `attendance:manage` is already Principal + Office exactly, so the owner's scoping needed no new permission. So AR adds no model, no migration, no vocab and no contract sync — one service, two queries, one screen. **AR-1:** `AttendanceRankingService` — window resolution (Saturday–Friday school week, calendar month, year-start→anchor, whole year), present % of held days, competition ranking (ties share a rank), and a 10-held-day floor that badges and sorts thin records last instead of letting a 3-day perfect record outrank a 60-day 98%. Students with ZERO absences are sourced from the roster, because absent-only capture never names them — the ranking would otherwise omit exactly the students who top it. Staff: approved LEAVE excluded from the denominator, LATE counted present but carried separately as the tie-breaker. **AR-2:** one `attendance:manage` screen — students/staff toggle, window chips, date, and axis chips (whole school / class / section / Quran / Arabic / one group), each row showing rank, present %, held days and absences. Held days are never hidden: a rank off four days is not the same claim as a rank off sixty. Gate: server tsc, app tsc, vocab verifier PASS (untouched), server jest **3207/3207** (180 suites, +20 new covering week/month/leap-year boundaries, the held-day denominator, the zero-absence student, rank ties, the floor, register separation and every staff status rule), expo web export exit 0.
- 2026-08-15 | Fix | **Three live defects from the owner's first real use of the attendance ranking (D-#493/#494).** **(1) Stat chips rendered one character per line** across the Today screen and the ranking rows. Root cause measured on the running app, not guessed: `Badge` carried `flexShrink: 1` plus `maxWidth: 50%`, and a percentage resolves against the PARENT — so a badge wrapped in a Pressable (which is itself sized by the badge) was capped at half of itself, 38px inside a 77px button, and its label then wrapped at every character. Badges are now unshrinkable with no percentage cap by default (the sibling text column already flexes, which is what actually protects it) and pinned to one line so a genuinely oversized label ellipsises rather than stacking. **(2) The Week window returned nothing.** The school week is Sunday–Thursday — across 32 marked dates on prod, Fri and Sat carry ZERO rows — so the Saturday-start week shipped in AR-1 put a Saturday anchor at the head of the week AHEAD; asking for "this week" on a day off showed five unmarked future days. Weeks now run Sun→Sat, so a Friday/Saturday anchor reports the school week that just finished. **(3) "So far" and "Annual" returned nothing.** Prod carries future-dated planning years (2027, 2029) and the window resolver took the newest by `startDate`, landing on 2029; it now follows `AcademicYear.current`. Rankings also carry `lastMarkedKey` so an empty window says the last day actually marked instead of reading as "nobody attended". Gate: server tsc, app tsc, vocab verifier PASS, server jest **3212/3212** (180 suites, +5 new pinning the Saturday-anchor and current-year regressions), expo web export exit 0 — and **verified at the running app**: badge labels measured at their full 61–88px widths with zero letter-stacks at both 1280px and 360px, and the four windows driven against the local register (week 2026-07-05→2026-07-11 from a Saturday anchor; cumulative and annual returning 91 rows).
- 2026-08-15 | Fix | **Attendance-ranking rows now say WHICH class (owner question: "why is KG showing 30 days but Nursery 32?").** The label was the section name alone, and every class's default section is named মূল (D-#1) — so a whole-school ranking rendered মূল on every row and a Nursery row was indistinguishable from a class-4 one. Rows are now labelled `class · section` (কেজি · মূল). The held-day difference itself was NOT a defect: held days are the days a unit actually marked, and on prod KG marked 30 while Nursery marked 32 — KG's markers missed exactly two days Nursery recorded (2026-06-21 and 2026-07-01). The same query surfaced something the school will care about more: classes 1–5 hold only **8** marked days each and none has marked since **2026-07-12**, so only Nursery and KG are still taking attendance and every class-1–5 student sits under the 10-day floor. Gate: server tsc, app tsc, server jest **3214/3214** (180 suites, +2 new pinning the label and the legitimately-differing denominators).
- 2026-08-15 | Correction | **The attendance ranking was measuring the wrong register for classes 1–5 — and an earlier CHANGELOG/STATUS claim from today was wrong because of it (D-#495).** Owner correction: "the attendance is taken on quran class and then sorted to c1-c5". That is exactly right and it is the module's documented design (D-#278, live 2026-07-13, `docs/prd-attendance-firstclass.md`): attendance is taken in the FIRST class of the day, which for classes 1–5 is a cross-section Quran `SubjectGroup`, so their day record carries `subjectGroupId`; Nursery/KG stay section-keyed; and every date before the cutover is section-shaped for everyone (D-#292). AR-1's school/class/section axes counted SECTION rows, so classes 1–5 surfaced with 8 held days — their last pre-cutover section marks — and **an entry earlier today claimed classes 1–5 "have not marked attendance since 2026-07-12". That claim was false**: they moved capture units on exactly that date. The section-shaped axes now resolve each student's unit per date, the way `absenteeReport` always has via `resolveUnits`, with the legacy cutover branch honoured; the Quran/Arabic axes still read the group register directly, which is the owner's explicit group-wise analysis. Display is unchanged — rows stay `class · section` and no group is ever surfaced. Re-checked against prod: every class now resolves to **31 held days** (Nursery 32, KG 30) with **no student at zero**, versus 8 for classes 1–5 before. Gate: server tsc, jest **3219/3219** (180 suites, +5 new covering group-captured counting, the cutover boundary in one window, Nursery/KG staying section-captured, the no-membership fallback, and two students of one section on different Quran groups keeping separate denominators).
- 2026-08-15 | Contract | **Import contract v1.0 -> v1.1: batch import via `doc_type: "question_batch"` (Principal ruling 2026-08-15, D-#496).** scd-central exports question banks as envelopes; single envelopes import clean today, but a 110-item bank meant 110 uploads. v1.1 adds a WRAPPER doc-type carrying `batch` metadata + an `items` array of standard question envelopes -- purely additive, so **every v1.0 shape validates byte-identically** and `envelope_version` stays the string `"1.0"` (the same additive rule that kept question/stimulus at 1.0; the *document* is v1.1, the wire field is not, and `"1.1"` is not an accepted `envelope_version`). Making the wrapper fit a schema whose root `required` list and `additionalProperties:false` assumed a single content doc meant moving the v1.0 required set into a conditional branch: non-batch doc-types re-require it verbatim AND are barred from `batch`/`items`, while question_batch requires `(batch, items)` and is barred from every single-envelope field. Three-place sync done (schema + `shared/vocab.ts` DOC_TYPES/labels + the harness, new L1b pass). **Whole-batch rejection on exactly three conditions** (items absent/empty, `item_count` != `items.length`, or >500 items -- the size guard), plus nesting; **everything else fails per element**, and the `items` element marker in the schema is deliberately the loosest possible one because tightening it silently converts per-item failures into whole-batch rejections -- a trap found by test, not by reading: an element missing `envelope_version` originally sank its 109 healthy siblings. Each element takes the UNCHANGED single-envelope path (`runImportGate` -> `persistEnvelope`); no new per-item validation logic exists. **Duplicate handling was READ off the live path, not assumed** -- `persistEnvelope`'s R-C7 block keys a question on `{docType:"question", envelopeJson.payload.qid, current:true}`, flips a matching current row to `current:false` and creates a NEW artifact with `priorVersionId`: a re-imported qid is a **version bump**, never an overwrite and never a second live row, reported as `imported` + `superseded:true`. One `batchId` per upload, stamped on every item's artifact (`importBatchId`) and on each element's audit row (`parentBatchId`). Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared build, shared+server tsc, server jest **3223/3223** (181 suites, +9 new) -- the batch suite deliberately does NOT mock the Python harness, so all 110 envelopes ran the real `validate_import.py`, built by the real `build_question_envelopes.py` from the committed LOCKED examples: 110 imported under one batchId; re-import -> 110 superseded (220 rows, 110 live); one corrupted element -> 109 imported + 1 failed with its REF19 reason; `item_count` off by one -> whole batch rejected, zero rows written; single-envelope import untouched.
- 2026-08-15 | Perf | **question_batch import is ~4.6x faster: the gate AND persistence now fan out, bounded, without changing a single verdict (D-#496).** Measured on the running local server before touching anything: a 110-item batch took **72s**, and the first fix only got it to 52s -- because the bottleneck was not what it looked like. Gating (one Python interpreter spawned per element) was ~30s of it and parallelises trivially, since a gate call is pure: temp file in, subprocess, stdout out. The REMAINING ~42s was 110 x ~6 sequential Mongo round-trips in the persist loop. So persistence fans out too -- but NOT naively: superseding is a read-then-write (`findOne current:true` -> `updateOne current:false` -> `create`), so two elements sharing a version key would race and could leave two live rows or a broken `priorVersionId` chain. The batch is therefore PARTITIONED by the exact version key `persistEnvelope` looks up (qid / stimulus_id / the address tuple): partitions run concurrently, each partition runs strictly in upload order. Elements with different keys provably cannot touch the same rows. Two caps, both env-overridable: `IMPORT_GATE_CONCURRENCY` (default cores-1, clamped 2..12 -- an unbounded `Promise.all` over a 500-item batch would try to run 500 interpreters and get SLOWER) and `IMPORT_PERSIST_CONCURRENCY` (default 8 -- these are round-trips, so the limit is the pool, not the CPU). Verdicts are assembled by walking the ORIGINAL index, never completion order. Gate: server tsc, server jest **3225/3225** (181 suites, +2 new pinning that verdict order survives a mid-batch failure under the parallel gate, and that a duplicate qid INSIDE one batch still supersedes in order leaving one live row); re-measured live at **15.6s** for the same 110 items with byte-identical verdicts, and the DB re-checked after 9 batch imports: 1100 rows, 209 live, **209 distinct qids -- no duplicate live rows**, every batch row still carrying exactly 110 child audit rows.
- 2026-08-15 | Feature | **Import screen surfaces per-element batch results instead of a bare tally (D-#496).** The server has returned `batchItems { qid status reason artifactId superseded }` since the v1.1 slice, but the app rendered only `itemsPassed/itemsTotal` + batchId -- so a 110-item upload with one bad element looked IDENTICAL to a clean one, and the importer had no way to learn which qid failed or why. Since a batch is deliberately NOT all-or-nothing, a PASS verdict can legitimately hide failures, so failed items are now shown **unconditionally** with their reason, the full per-item list sits behind a toggle (a 500-row dump would bury exactly the rows worth acting on), and a supersede count says how many qids became new versions rather than fresh rows. Adds a pre-import notice when a picked file is a `question_batch` wrapper -- distinct from the existing question-BANK notice, which is what the owner first uploaded by mistake; a batch needs no curation tag, because every element is already a built envelope carrying its own. New BN+EN label pairs; both import mutations now request the batch fields through one shared fragment constant. Gate: app tsc clean, expo web export exit 0 (checked directly, not through a pipe), server jest 3225/3225.

- 2026-08-16 — **feat(assignment): the gift report evaluates continuously instead of at week end (D-#498).** Owner opened the shipped screen on prod mid-week and saw an empty page: the first cut judged a week only after its due date passed, which correctly avoided reporting the whole school as failing while records sit in DUE, but also hid the students who had genuinely finished — and gave no way to tell "nobody qualified" from "nothing entered yet". Replaced the two-state settled gate with four outcomes per (student × week): WON (settled, nothing outstanding), QUALIFIED (week still LIVE but everything already in — counts as a win now, streak increments, handover permitted, flagged provisional), PENDING (live with work unmarked — neither win nor loss, does NOT reset a streak), LOST (settled with something outstanding). New "waiting on entries" card naming each student's outstanding subjects. QUALIFIED is provisional because confirmAssignmentWeek re-runs for new DRAFT items, so a teacher can add a subject mid-week and raise issued under a student who had qualified — verified in the service, not assumed; that same re-entrancy is why every stored award now carries entitlementHolds, re-derived per read and flagged on screen when it goes false rather than silently rewritten. Handover refusal on a still-pending week says "data not in yet", a different message from "the student missed one". Numbering note: D-#484 was taken by a concurrent ACS-1 session between pre-flight and append; renumbered to D-#498 against the live log (AGENTS rule 3). Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared+server+app tsc clean, server jest 3237/3237 (181 suites, +7 new live-week tests), expo web export exit 0 (checked directly, not through a pipe).
- 2026-08-16 | Feature | **The v1.1 batch feature is now PRODUCIBLE: `build_question_envelopes.py --batch` emits the wrapper (D-#498).** v1.1 shipped the consumer half — the app and harness understood `question_batch`, but NOTHING emitted one, so the feature was live and dormant: the builder wrote a bare JSON array of N envelopes and the only ways in were still N single uploads or the legacy atomic bank fan-out. `--batch` wraps the SAME envelopes it already builds into one `question_batch`, with `--bank-id` (defaulting to the source filename stem) and `--bank-version` (default v1). `item_count` is COMPUTED, not accepted as a flag — a producer-authored count that disagrees with the array is precisely the whole-batch rejection the contract defines, so there is no reason to let anyone type it — and `digest` is a real sha256 over the canonical items JSON (recorded at import, never recomputed). Per the owner's ruling a batch carries whatever the bank produced, **stimuli included**: a bank is `{stimuli, questions}` and is batched whole; stimulus elements take the same unchanged single path and supersede on `stimulus_id`. Without `--batch` the output is byte-identical to before, so the pre-v1.1 flow is untouched. Gate: server tsc, server jest **3231/3231** (181 suites, +1 new that runs the REAL builder with `--batch` over the committed LOCKED examples and imports the result — asserting the computed count, the sha256 shape, and that a MIXED stimulus+question batch imports with per-element verdicts keyed by `stimulus_id` and `qid`); verified live end-to-end: a 2-element mixed batch imported PASS 2/2, and the 110-question bank re-emitted through `--batch` produced a wrapper whose `item_count` matched and whose digest was stable.

- 2026-08-16 — **fix(daily-entry): a class note with an attachment could not be published when the card also declared homework (D-#499).** Owner on prod: publish works with no attachment, fails with one, on `Every attachment must be an uploaded homework question file`. The daily-entry card has ONE attachment picker on purpose (DE-3/D-#477 merged the surfaces), it uploads via `POST /files/classnote` → kind `classnote_attachment`, and the same ids go to both the note and the homework it declares — but `HomeworkService.normalizeAttachmentIds` still matched `kind: "hw_question"` exactly (D-#297, written when homework had its own picker), so it found 0 of 1 and threw. D-#477 runs homework BEFORE the note upsert, so the throw pre-empted every write — hence all-or-nothing rather than a partial publish. Widened the bind to `HW_BINDABLE_FILE_KINDS = [hw_question, classnote_attachment]`; changing what the picker uploads would only move the failure, since the class-note side kind-checks nothing at all. `hw_answer` and chat files stay excluded, which is what the check was actually built for. No read-gate, schema, contract or app change. Numbering note: D-#498 was taken by PR #593 (`question_batch` builder) between pre-flight and merge; renumbered to D-#499 against the live log (AGENTS rule 3). Gate: server tsc clean (needs `--max-old-space-size=8192`; the default heap OOMs on this repo), server jest **3238 passed / 3245 total, 180 of 182 suites** (+1 new test pinning the class-note kind as bindable). - 2026-08-16 | Fix | **The Class-test dashboard was making thousands of database round trips to read a one-row table (D-#500).** Owner report: the page sits on "Loading…". Root cause measured, not guessed: `resolveDayType` costs one `HolidayException.findOne` per call, and `buildIsOpenDay` called it **once per day** across a ~70-day window — **once per exam**. `reportsStatus` then looped every exam calling `examReportStatus`, which rebuilt that whole calendar from scratch each time and re-read the test row it already held. Measured on FIVE local exams: `reportsStatus` **75,046 ms / 265 db ops** (224 on `holidayexceptions`), `principalDashboard` **22,381 ms / 241**, `overdueChaseList` **22,334 ms / 243** — and the screen fires all three. Prod has 43 printed exams. **Fixed** by adding `buildDayTypeResolver(from, to)` (one query for a whole range, holidays pre-expanded to a covered-day set) plus `buildIsOpenDayForRange`, and by batching `reportsStatus`: one shared calendar for the entire exam set, one roster aggregate per section, one status aggregate for all exams, and the per-exam math extracted to a PURE `examStatusFrom` so no exam costs a round trip. `examReportStatus` is untouched and remains the single-exam entry point. **After: 1,647 ms / 26 ops, 746 ms / 13, 982 ms / 10** — ~45× faster, 749 round trips down to 49. **Equivalence proven, not assumed:** a harness compared every field of the batched rows against the untouched per-exam `examReportStatus` for every exam on the live copy — identical on all 12 fields including the two overdue exams whose `schoolDaysLate` (22 and 21) depends entirely on the calendar. Gate: server tsc, vocab verifier PASS, jest **3245/3245** (182 suites, +7 new pinning the range resolver — one query per range, multi-day and mid-day holiday spans, agreement with `resolveDayType` across a fortnight, out-of-window fallback — plus a regression test that one roster read serves the whole exam set). Two existing suites had their mocks migrated: they mocked the per-exam collaborator that no longer runs, so their fixtures now express the same intent as roster/entered counts, and the chase-message byte-check asserts the calendar's own lateness rather than an injected number.
- 2026-08-16 — **feat(assignment): the gift report evaluates continuously instead of at week end (D-#497).** Owner opened the shipped screen on prod mid-week and saw an empty page: the first cut judged a week only after its due date passed, which correctly avoided reporting the whole school as failing while records sit in DUE, but also hid the students who had genuinely finished — and gave no way to tell "nobody qualified" from "nothing entered yet". Replaced the two-state settled gate with four outcomes per (student × week): WON (settled, nothing outstanding), QUALIFIED (week still LIVE but everything already in — counts as a win now, streak increments, handover permitted, flagged provisional), PENDING (live with work unmarked — neither win nor loss, does NOT reset a streak), LOST (settled with something outstanding). New "waiting on entries" card naming each student's outstanding subjects. QUALIFIED is provisional because confirmAssignmentWeek re-runs for new DRAFT items, so a teacher can add a subject mid-week and raise issued under a student who had qualified — verified in the service, not assumed; that same re-entrancy is why every stored award now carries entitlementHolds, re-derived per read and flagged on screen when it goes false rather than silently rewritten. Handover refusal on a still-pending week says "data not in yet", a different message from "the student missed one". Numbering note: D-#484 was taken by a concurrent ACS-1 session between pre-flight and append; renumbered to D-#497 against the live log (AGENTS rule 3). Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared+server+app tsc clean, server jest 3237/3237 (181 suites, +7 new live-week tests), expo web export exit 0 (checked directly, not through a pipe).
- 2026-08-16 — **feat(routine): create and retire Quran/Arabic groups in the UI (D-#501).** Owner asked how to split the combined Hifz 1 group into boys/girls and rebuild the routine; pre-flight found two of the four steps had no UI. `createSubjectGroup` had existed since R1.4 wired to NO screen (grep in app/src returned zero hits — every group in the picker was seeded), and retiring a group was impossible at any layer: `SubjectGroup.active` was declared on the model and written by nothing, while `subjectGroups` ignored it. Added: a create-group form on Admin → Group members (track/gender chips, level + Bangla name, code auto-derived as TRACK_LEVEL_GENDER and editable, normalised + uniqueness-checked server-side with a Bangla message instead of a raw duplicate-key error); `setSubjectGroupActive`; `includeInactive` on the group query, filtering `{ $ne: false }` so pre-existing rows without the field stay visible. Retiring is REFUSED while members remain and names the count — `addGroupMember` enforces one-group-per-track by scanning every group of that track regardless of active, so a retired-but-populated group would silently block its own students from joining the group replacing it. Restore is unguarded (only retiring creates the trap). Reuses routine:manage; no new permission, no vocab edit. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared+server+app tsc clean, server jest 3250/3250 (182 suites, +13 new), expo web export exit 0 (checked directly, not through a pipe).

- 2026-08-16 | Feature | **The v1.1 batch feature is now PRODUCIBLE: `build_question_envelopes.py --batch` emits the wrapper (D-#498).** v1.1 shipped the consumer half — the app and harness understood `question_batch`, but NOTHING emitted one, so the feature was live and dormant: the builder wrote a bare JSON array of N envelopes and the only ways in were still N single uploads or the legacy atomic bank fan-out. `--batch` wraps the SAME envelopes it already builds into one `question_batch`, with `--bank-id` (defaulting to the source filename stem) and `--bank-version` (default v1). `item_count` is COMPUTED, not accepted as a flag — a producer-authored count that disagrees with the array is precisely the whole-batch rejection the contract defines, so there is no reason to let anyone type it — and `digest` is a real sha256 over the canonical items JSON (recorded at import, never recomputed). Per the owner's ruling a batch carries whatever the bank produced, **stimuli included**: a bank is `{stimuli, questions}` and is batched whole; stimulus elements take the same unchanged single path and supersede on `stimulus_id`. Without `--batch` the output is byte-identical to before, so the pre-v1.1 flow is untouched. Gate: server tsc, server jest **3231/3231** (181 suites, +1 new that runs the REAL builder with `--batch` over the committed LOCKED examples and imports the result — asserting the computed count, the sha256 shape, and that a MIXED stimulus+question batch imports with per-element verdicts keyed by `stimulus_id` and `qid`); verified live end-to-end: a 2-element mixed batch imported PASS 2/2, and the 110-question bank re-emitted through `--batch` produced a wrapper whose `item_count` matched and whose digest was stable.

- 2026-08-16 — **fix(daily-entry): a class note with an attachment could not be published when the card also declared homework (D-#499).** Owner on prod: publish works with no attachment, fails with one, on `Every attachment must be an uploaded homework question file`. The daily-entry card has ONE attachment picker on purpose (DE-3/D-#477 merged the surfaces), it uploads via `POST /files/classnote` → kind `classnote_attachment`, and the same ids go to both the note and the homework it declares — but `HomeworkService.normalizeAttachmentIds` still matched `kind: "hw_question"` exactly (D-#297, written when homework had its own picker), so it found 0 of 1 and threw. D-#477 runs homework BEFORE the note upsert, so the throw pre-empted every write — hence all-or-nothing rather than a partial publish. Widened the bind to `HW_BINDABLE_FILE_KINDS = [hw_question, classnote_attachment]`; changing what the picker uploads would only move the failure, since the class-note side kind-checks nothing at all. `hw_answer` and chat files stay excluded, which is what the check was actually built for. No read-gate, schema, contract or app change. Numbering note: D-#498 was taken by PR #593 (`question_batch` builder) between pre-flight and merge; renumbered to D-#499 against the live log (AGENTS rule 3). Gate: server tsc clean (needs `--max-old-space-size=8192`; the default heap OOMs on this repo), server jest **3238 passed / 3245 total, 180 of 182 suites** (+1 new test pinning the class-note kind as bindable). The 7 failures are NOT clean and are stated as such: all sit in `classTestSummary`/`classTestResult`, timing out on `holidayexceptions.find()` from unrelated uncommitted class-test work in the working tree (not part of this commit); neither suite references HomeworkService or attachments, and all 18 homework/class-note suites pass.

- 2026-08-16 — **fix(routine): the effective window is day-granular, so a slot effective TODAY is live today for every reader (D-#502).** Owner hit this splitting Hifz 1 on prod: a Quran group's routine slot created with "effective from = today" appeared in the routine editor's Existing-slots list but the attendance marker resolved to "Nobody assigned", so nobody could mark that group. Cause: `liveWindow`/`isLiveOn` compared raw INSTANTS, and the two ends are built differently — `effectiveFrom` is stored by `new Date("YYYY-MM-DD")` (UTC midnight) while readers pass `new Date()` (now) OR a LOCAL-midnight Date (attendance's `parseDateKey`). On the UTC+6 prod server local midnight is 18:00Z the previous day, earlier than the slot's 00:00Z, so the same slot was live for one reader and dead for the other. Verified numerically under TZ=Asia/Dhaka before changing anything (old rule false, new rule true). Fixed on the READ side — `effectiveFrom <= endOfDay(on)`, `effectiveTo >= startOfDay(on)` — so rows already in the database are repaired without re-saving every slot; new `endOfDay` helper; the eight inlined copies of the predicate across MyDay/RoutineCover/RoutineSlot/RoutineTrigger now call `liveWindow()` instead of restating it (TeacherClassLoad keeps its own — it is a month-range overlap, not a live-on-a-day test). Changeover semantics unchanged and still tested (`endOfDayBefore` still leaves exactly one row live on a handover day). One pre-existing assertion updated: it required `$lte` to be identical to the passed instant, which pinned the removed behaviour. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, server+app tsc clean, server jest 3274/3274 (184 suites, +13 new boundary tests) run under BOTH the default TZ and TZ=Asia/Dhaka, expo web export exit 0 (checked directly, not through a pipe).
- 2026-08-17 — **feat(observation): an optional "Overall suggestion" box on the classroom-review form (CO-16, D-#503).** Reviewer feedback after watching a class-2 Math recording: the useful thought was "these students have a lot of energy — channel it into writing, pair/group work, visual materials, hands-on modelling (do the money problem in pairs with the school's fake notes)", and the REF-11 form had nowhere to put it. Every free-text box on that form hangs off something scored (a domain note, a breach note, one strength, the growth focus, the CO-10 progress note), so a cross-cutting idea had to be filed as a domain weakness — or left unsaid. Adds ONE optional field `overallSuggestion`: validated with the rest of the REF-11 payload (trimmed, null when blank, never required), in its own card AFTER the scored block with help text saying it is added to no score, carried by the local draft autosave, shown on the observation detail and per reviewer on the CO-9 compare view, and released to the observed teacher with the rest of the review on PUBLISH (CO-8) — never before. Read by nothing that computes (CO-4 trend / CO-6 tier / CO-7 effectiveness / CO-13 ranking take levels, gates and fairness ratings only), so making a suggestion can never cost a teacher a mark. REF-11 only — the Quran (ClassEcho) form already asks for `quran.suggestions` (CO-5) and ignores this field. No vocab/enum change, no contract sync, no migration (defaults null). Gates: server jest 3285/3285 (185 suites, 14 new), server + app tsc clean, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-17 — **feat(class-notes): "All class notes" — the filtered, paginated archive (D-#504).** Owner: every note should be visible, filterable class / section / subject / teacher / date, 50 to a page, in a table with edit and delete. Class Notes becomes a drawer GROUP — "আমার পিরিয়ড" (the unchanged teacher day screen) and "সব ক্লাস নোট" (the new archive). Server: `classNotesPage` pages with `skip`/`limit` + `countDocuments` (never fetch-then-slice — an archive grows for the life of the school), `classNoteFilterOptions` returns only values that exist in the caller's slice, and the row now carries `sectionId` / `classId` / `authorId`. The class filter widens to that class's sections, because a note stores its section, not its class. **Scope is server-side:** `routine:manage` sees the school and may pick a teacher; anyone else is pinned to their own notes and a forged `teacherId` is ignored. `updateClassNote` moves to `routine:read` + an in-service `publishedBy` check, so a teacher may edit the note they authored (the permission the publish upsert already gave them, D-#336) and nobody else's; delete stays `routine:manage`. The columns the owner listed carry no note text, so pressing a row opens a band with the summary and its attachment links. The report-table "paper" palette moved to `theme/reportPaper.ts` and is now shared with ClassNoteReportScreen rather than copied. Gates (executed): server tsc, app tsc, server jest **3295/3295 (186 suites, +10 new)** covering skip/limit, the class→sections widening, the both-ends-inclusive date window, a non-ObjectId filter matching nothing, the pageSize clamp, scoped options, and the author edit gate; then driven in the running app (Edge/Playwright, principal + a real teacher token): 66 notes → page 1 ১–৫০, page 2 ৫১–৬৬ with zero overlap, subject → 10, +class → 2, teacher → 6, reset → 66, row band and edit form open; a teacher saw only their own 3 notes, a forged `teacherId` changed nothing, editing their own note succeeded and was restored, editing another teacher's was refused ("You can only edit your own class note"), delete was refused outright, and the archive total was unchanged afterwards.
- 2026-08-17 — **feat(guardian): the homework screen answers the DAY — one card per date, every subject, three honest states (GP-9, D-#506).** Owner review of a real class-1 family: the date was a small grey line above per-subject cards, and every "no homework" declaration for the whole range sat in ONE card at the top — so for a given day a parent could not tell "no homework" from "nobody said anything". Now: one card per day with the date as its bold title; every subject that had a period that day, in period order, showing either the full homework lifecycle, or the teacher's "বাড়ির কাজ নেই" with its reason, or `ঘোষণা করা হয়নি` (deliberately NOT worded as "no homework" — an unanswered subject is a different fact). The range-wide nil card is gone. New guardian read `childRoutineRange(studentId, from, to)`: the same narrow day shape as `childRoutine` (D-#69, no teacher/room/cover key), for a whole window in a FIXED number of queries (holidays + slots + schedule windows + period grids loaded once, the day loop pure, per-day effective window via the day-granular `isLiveOn`, D-#502) — a `childRoutine`-per-day loop would have restored the ~90-request fan-out D-#476 removed. QURAN periods are excluded (Quran homework is the Quran tracker's channel, D-#36); anything declared for a subject with no period that day is still shown. The lesson-history day card now lists declared items AND nil declarations together instead of hiding the latter behind an if/else-if. Gates: server jest 3311/3311 (187 suites; +16 tests from this change, re-run after merging origin/dev), server + app tsc clean, expo web export exit 0.
- 2026-08-17 — **feat(classnotes): the daily-entry card warns when the lesson text announces homework nobody is declaring (D-#505).** One class-1 day carried three notes reading "H.W-Learn the 12 months names", "hw-প ও ফ দিয়ে…", "H.w-- ঌ-া দিয়ে…" while exactly ONE homework item was declared — and the English teacher had declared "no homework (revision)" for the very period they had written homework into. Prose in a note is not a declaration (no question count, no time, no due date) and cannot be promoted into one without fabricating all three, so the card now asks the human while they are still there: text mentioning `H.W`/`hw`/`বাড়ির কাজ`/`হোমওয়ার্ক` with বাড়ির কাজ set to নেই (or unanswered) shows a Bangla reminder that undeclared homework never reaches the guardian's list. It never blocks — a note may legitimately mention last week's work. The predicate is a pure RN-free module (`app/src/lib/homeworkText.ts`) unit-tested from the server suite; a bare "h w" is deliberately not matched so the warning stays credible.
- 2026-08-17 — **fix(guardian): the routine card on the guardian home screen is titled "আজকের রুটিন", not "আজ" (owner ask).** The screen itself is "আজ", so a card inside it repeating that title said nothing about what the card holds. New `gpTodayRoutine` label (BN + EN); the drawer item and screen title are unchanged.

- 2026-08-17 — **feat(class-notes): the note itself is a column in the archive, expandable per row (owner ask, refines D-#504).** The owner, reading the shipped table on prod: can the class note be shown in the table, with expand/shrink? It was reachable but invisible — the body only appeared in a detail band after pressing a row, with nothing on screen saying so. Now `বিষয়বস্তু` is the 4th column (after class, beside what identifies the note — not stranded past the columns nobody reads a note by), clamped to two lines with a `▸`/`▾` caret; pressing anywhere on the row unclamps that row and reveals its attachment links, and a `সব খুলুন` / `সব বন্ধ করুন` button does the whole page at once. Rows stay short by default so 50 of them are still scannable — a table that opens every note by default is a list, not a table. Expansion state is a Set of row ids (was a single id), which is what makes expand-all meaningful. App-only: no query, resolver, permission or schema change; the note text was already on the row payload. Gates: app tsc clean, and driven in the running app — the header reads তারিখ · বিষয় · ক্লাস · বিষয়বস্তু · পোস্ট করেছেন · সংযুক্তি · শাখা · কার্যক্রম; expand-all opened all 50 (a two-paragraph C.W/H.W note rendering in full, a 2-file note showing both attachment links), collapse-all returned every row to the two-line clamp, and one row toggled alone 50→49/1→50 without touching its neighbours.
- 2026-08-17 — **feat(class-test): an Arabic class test can be recorded for its GROUP, not only a section (CT-12, D-#507).** Owner asked whether Arabic tests can be recorded like classes 1–5. Measured answer: of 37 active ARABIC routine slots **25 are group-shaped** across five cross-class groups (`ARABIC_BOOK_2_GIRLS` has 11 members from 4 different classes), and **zero** Arabic class tests existed — a `ClassTest` required a `sectionId` and counted that section's roster, so a group exam could only be filed by pretending it belonged to one section, which then listed children who never attend the group and left the members from other classes unreachable. Now `ClassTest` carries EXACTLY ONE of `sectionId` / `subjectGroupId` (the ClassroomObservation anchor shape): a group exam has null class/section, takes the current academic year, mints `CT-G-{GROUP_CODE}-{nnnn}` from a new `ClassTestGroupSequence` (separate collection — a nullable `classLevel` would collide on the live unique index), and its roster + completion denominator are the group's active members. Write scope for a group exam is "the routine names you on that group" (teacher scopes are section grants and cannot answer for a cross-class unit), PRINCIPAL passes, OFFICE does not; `enterResult` refuses a non-member; copies-per-present is refused (no single class to count); the CT-11 duplicate guard keys on the anchor; only ARABIC-track groups qualify (D-#36 keeps Quran out). New `classTestRoster` query serves the marks screen for both anchors and shows each child's class·section on a group exam; the request form gets a শাখা / আরবি গ্রুপ chip; the guardian card names the group instead of a class it was not held for. Section-keyed CT-4 dashboards stay section-keyed (group rosters are counted correctly and the chase line names the group; the class/section filters do not offer groups yet). No vocab change, no new permission, no migration (`subjectGroupId` defaults null).

- 2026-08-18 — **docs(questions): PRD for the question review & publish loop — the bank becomes a vetted shelf (D-#508).** Owner asked for a review step on the question feature: the Principal assigns questions to a teacher, who accepts or rejects with an OPTIONAL reason; accepted questions return to the Principal and only once he publishes them can teachers use them. Pre-flighted against the live code: the plan review loop already ships everything except the gate — `ReviewAssignment`, assign/bulk/submit/cancel/inbox/thread, the reviewer read-scope override, audit, notifications, and a sign-off that already implements "publish anyway, but state a reason" — and all three permissions already exist with the right grants, so there is NO vocab change and NO two-place contract sync. Three findings shaped the plan: questions supersede on `qid` not the address (so a plan-style thread key would have put 40 questions on one thread and cancelled 39 unrelated rounds), the envelope schema permits `review_status: "gold"` and the importer stored it verbatim (an upload could self-publish), and a question's `stimulus_ref` means gating stimuli would render published questions without their passage. Measured the bank read-only before ruling on the clearing step: prod holds 6,901 question documents, 6,505 current, **all `draft`, none `gold`** — so the gate alone empties the teachers' bank without deleting anything; the owner was shown this and chose the hard delete regardless, as a separate owner-confirmed operational step (OPS-1) after the gate ships, never in the feature PR. New: `docs/prd-question-review.md` (slices QR-1..QR-4 + OPS-1, journeys Q1.1–Q5.5, 3 settled items, 0 open). Planning only — no code touched, nothing built, no gate run beyond the read-only count (`server/scripts/diag-question-bank-size.ts`, untracked).

- 2026-08-18 — **feat(content): QR-1 — a question's review thread anchors on its `qid`, and an import can no longer publish itself (D-#508).** Three surgical changes. (1) `ReviewAssignment` gains a nullable `qid` + a sparse `{qid,status}` index, and `ReviewService` gains `qidOf` / `threadKeyOf` / `supersedeOpenRoundsForQid`; `supersedeOpenRoundsForAddress` is now a thin wrapper over a shared `supersedeOpenRounds`, so plan behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged. The anchor matters because `persistEnvelope` supersedes questions on `envelopeJson.payload.qid` precisely because **a whole unit of questions shares one address** — anchoring rounds on the address would have put every question in the unit on one thread and let a single supersede cancel dozens of unrelated rounds. `threadKeyOf` REFUSES a question with no qid rather than silently falling back to the address, which is the exact bug it exists to prevent. (2) `persistEnvelope`'s question branch — until now a no-op behind an `isPlanDocType` guard — supersedes open rounds by qid on re-import. (3) **Questions are clamped to `draft` at import** whatever the envelope declares: the schema permits `review_status: "gold"`, so without this an upload could publish straight onto the teachers' shelf and skip the review the loop exists to enforce. Plans keep honouring the declared value (`content.test.ts` still asserts `reviewed` for a `session_plan`), and the `ImportBatch` audit row still records what ARRIVED, not the clamp. New suite `questionReview.test.ts` (Q1.1/Q1.2) + 5 tests in `questions.test.ts` (Q1.3 + the re-import wiring). Gate: server tsc clean, vocab verifier PASS (proving the no-vocab-change claim), **jest 3347/3347 across 189 suites**. No gate on reads or selection yet — that is QR-3; nothing a teacher sees changes in this slice.

- 2026-08-18 — **feat(questions): QR-2 + QR-3 — the question review loop, and the publish gate that makes it mean something (D-#508).** QR-2 adds `QuestionReviewService` + `questionReview` resolvers: `assignQuestionReview(Bulk)`, `submitQuestionReview`, `publishQuestion(Bulk)`, `myQuestionReviews`, `questionReviewInbox(verdict)` (APPROVE = the publish queue, CHANGES_REQUESTED = the rejected list), `questionReviewThread`, `assignableQuestions`.
