Kept as its OWN service rather than a generalisation of `ReviewService` — the two loops diverge on the feedback rule, on what "published" means and on list shape, and the plan loop is shipped and in daily use — but the primitives that must not drift (`threadKeyOf`, `supersedeOpenRounds`, `reviewStatusForVerdict`, `toDTO`) are reused, not copied. **The rejection reason is OPTIONAL for questions** while `submitPlanReview` still throws without feedback; both are asserted in one test file so the divergence cannot be "unified" away by accident. Publishing reuses `approvePlan`'s two paths verbatim, so a REJECTED question can still be published only with a mandatory override reason, stamped and audited (new `QUESTION_PUBLISHED` eventKind). QR-3 then closes the seven ungated call sites: `questions`, `questionTopicTags`, `question(id)`, `contentArtifacts`, `contentTree`, `addQuestionToSet`/`createSetFromBasket`, and the homework top-up validator. The gate lives in ONE module (`modules/questions/publishGate.ts`) and is always a **Mongo predicate, never a post-filter** — `questions()` is cursor-paginated, so dropping rows afterwards would silently short every page. It is set AFTER any `reviewStatus` argument, so a teacher passing `reviewStatus: "draft"` cannot widen it back open. `question(id)` carries the one exemption: the assigned reviewer may READ the unpublished question they were asked to review (never select it — Q5.3). **Stimuli are deliberately NOT gated** (§5a): a question's `stimulus_ref` must always resolve, so gating shared passages would render a published question without its text; a test asserts the exemption so it cannot be tidied into line. Set rendering now shows a missing question as a visible `[প্রশ্নটি আর নেই]` gap instead of silently `continue`-ing, which had renumbered every later question. Gate: server tsc clean, vocab verifier PASS, **jest 3380/3380 across 191 suites** (9 pre-gate selection fixtures updated to publish their questions, which is the new reality). No app changes yet — that is QR-4.

- 2026-08-18 — **feat(app): QR-4 — the question review workspace: reviewer queue, assign, publish (D-#508).** Four screens on the existing Review stack. **Reviewer queue** renders each assigned question in place with **Accept / Reject on the card** — a reviewer works through dozens of questions, so a detail screen per question would be the wrong shape; the reject box is labelled `কারণ (ঐচ্ছিক)` and **Reject stays enabled while it is empty**, which is the Q2.4 divergence made visible. **Assign** filters by subject/class/search, multi-selects, picks a reviewer, and bulk-assigns — reporting `(n ✓, m ✗)` when the batch had partial failures rather than claiming a clean run. **Publish** carries two tabs: Accepted (multi-select → publish) and Rejected (each with the reviewer's reason, or `—` when none was given, plus "publish anyway" behind a **mandatory** reason box) — bulk publish deliberately carries no override reason, because an override is a per-question judgement. **Thread** shows a question's round history, anchored on its qid so it never bleeds into unit-mates. All four registered AFTER `ReviewHome` in the stack, since the first-registered screen becomes the initial route and a param-taking screen there crashes the whole tab. New BN+EN labels (`qr*`). Gate: server tsc + app tsc clean, vocab verifier PASS, **jest 3380/3380 (191 suites)**, **expo web export exit 0** (exit code checked directly, not through a pipe). **QR-1..QR-4 are now complete; OPS-1 — the 6,901-document delete — remains NOT started and is the owner's call to run.**

- 2026-08-18 — **fix(questions,content): three defects found by auditing QR-1..QR-4 against the PRD (D-#509).** (1) **The reviewer notification named the wrong thing.** `review.assigned.body` reads "**পরিকল্পনাটি** … নির্ধারিত হয়েছে" and quotes an address, but a question shares its unit address with dozens of others — so a teacher assigned one question was told a *plan* was waiting at an address identifying neither. New `question.review.assigned.*` keys (which narrows D-#508's "no vocab change" to "no permission/enum/wire-contract change"). (2) **Bulk assign spammed the reviewer** — assigning is bulk by design, so a per-round emit meant 40 questions → 40 pushes; the emit moved to the assign ACTION, once, with a `count`. (3) **Question rounds leaked into the plan surfaces.** Before QR-2 only plans created `ReviewAssignment` rows, so `listMyReviewAssignments`, `planReviewInbox` and `reviewerAssignmentLoad` never needed a docType filter; once the collection was shared, question rounds appeared in the teacher's PLAN queue (whose card opens a plan viewer and whose form demands feedback questions do not require), in the Principal's plan inbox, and in the "who has how many" count. All three now filter `PLAN_DOC_TYPES`; `planReviewThread` REFUSES a question outright, because resolving one keys on the shared address and returns every OTHER question in the unit — the exact bug the qid anchor exists to prevent. Also closed two PRD gaps the audit surfaced: **Q3.7** — an unfiltered empty bank now says "প্রকাশিত প্রশ্নই কেবল এখানে দেখা যায়" instead of telling the teacher to change filters they never set — and **Q5.1/Q5.3** — tests asserting D-#508 adds no permission, that publish stays Principal-locked while Office may assign, and that the assigned reviewer who may READ an unpublished question still cannot SELECT it. Gate: server + app tsc clean, vocab verifier PASS, **jest 3390/3390 (191 suites)**, expo web export exit 0.
- 2026-08-18 — **feat(guardian): the homework screen leads with what is still owed, and stops reporting Arabic as a gap (GP-10, D-#510).** Two corrections from the owner reading the shipped GP-9 card on a phone. **(1)** ARABIC printed `ঘোষণা করা হয়নি` every single day: Arabic homework is never declared in this channel — it is written into the class note — and the school had already ruled on it (`HW_DECLARATION_EXPECTED_SUBJECTS` excludes ARABIC, D-#308). The card now honours that ruling: a subject outside the expected set reads `ক্লাস নোটে দেখুন`, while the strict wording survives for the subjects that ARE expected, which is the only way it keeps meaning anything. **(2)** New outstanding card above the day log — homework in DUE/CHASE plus assignments chased or pending-and-late, each with the date it was given, its due date, and the work itself clamped to two lines with a press-to-expand caret (the AllClassNotes idiom); hidden entirely when nothing is outstanding. The outstanding rule is the home screen's `করতে হবে` rule verbatim (DE-6/D-#477) so the two surfaces cannot disagree, and resubmissions are excluded so one piece of work is never counted twice. The card reads its OWN 92-day window, deliberately not the date pickers': a card claiming "everything still pending" must not let a narrowed range hide last month's unsubmitted homework. App-only — no resolver, permission or schema change. Gates: app + server tsc clean, server jest green (4 new source guards in `guardianDayCard.test.ts`), expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-18 | Release | **Three features promoted to prod and shipped to phones by OTA** (update group `9dd37d4c`, channel `production`, runtime 1.1.0, android, Android sourcemaps uploaded): **CO-16** the optional "সামগ্রিক পরামর্শ" box on the classroom-review form (D-#503, PR #601 → #602); **GP-9** the guardian day view — one card per date, every subject of that day in one of three states, plus the declare-time homework-in-note reminder (D-#506/#505, PR #603 → #606); **CT-12** a class test anchored on an Arabic SUBJECT GROUP (D-#507, PR #609 → #610, prod deploy success, `/healthz` 200, live schema introspection shows `subjectGroupId`/`groupNameBn`/`ClassTestRosterStudent`). **The `app/.env` trap documented at `docs/app-distribution.md` fired again and was caught pre-publish:** the day-to-day checkout had `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL=http://localhost:4000/graphql`, which `eas update` does not check — publishing from that state would have pointed every phone at its own localhost. Overrode the URL to the prod https endpoint keeping the real Sentry DSN, published, restored the owner's dev value immediately, and then **verified the published Hermes bundle itself** rather than trusting the exit code: it contains `scdhub.shafayet.me/graphql`, contains no `localhost:4000/graphql`, and carries all three features' new strings (`ঘোষণা করা হয়নি`, `আজকের রুটিন`, `childRoutineRange`, `সামগ্রিক পরামর্শ`, `overallSuggestion`, `আরবি গ্রুপ`, `classTestRoster`). Phones apply it on the next cold start.

- 2026-08-22 — **feat(questions): the question bank can be filtered by exercise family, not just answer type (D-#511).** The C5 Bangla bank carries শব্দার্থ, বিপরীত শব্দ, সমার্থক শব্দ, এক কথায় প্রকাশ, ভাষারীতি, বাক্য গঠন, পদ নির্ণয়, ক্রিয়ার কাল, যুক্তবর্ণ বিভাজন and মূলভাব items that were previously indistinguishable — শব্দার্থ, বিপরীত and এক কথায় are all `short_answer` + `short` + 1 mark. New `QUESTION_CATEGORIES` vocab (app-native, no wire twin), a `category` filter arg + field on the question surface reading the payload's `lesson_ref`, and a `questionCategories` query whose emptiness is what hides the chip group, so it renders for the C5 Bangla slice and no other without hardcoding a subject. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, server + app tsc, server jest **3407/3407 (192 suites)** with 13 new, expo web export exit 0.
- 2026-08-22 — **feat(attendance): the ranking row names the student’s class, and the list can be sorted by it (AR-3, D-#512).** On the Quran/Arabic axes every row in a ranking carried the SAME `unitLabel` — the group name — because those groups are cross-grade by design (D-#48): a child is placed by Quran level, not by class. So a list of কায়দা attenders said nothing about whether a name belonged to a class-1 child or a class-5 one, which is the first question a cross-grade ranking invites. `studentAttendanceRanking` rows now carry `classLabel` + `classLevel`, resolved in one extra query for the whole set, and a new `sortBy` argument (`rank` default, or `class`) regroups the list by class. **Sorting by class never renumbers** — the same ranks travel with the rows, so a rank means the same thing under both sorts and the screen says so when the class sort is active; renumbering per class would make “1” mean group-winner in one view and class-winner in the other with nothing on the row to tell them apart. The section-shaped axes populate the field too, and the screen suppresses the badge where `unitLabel` already leads with the class rather than printing it twice. An unresolved class sorts last (`?? 99`, not `?? 0` — nursery is level -1 and KG is 0). No permission, enum or wire-contract change. Gate: server + app tsc clean, **server jest 3399/3399 (191 suites)** with 5 new (the cross-grade join, the reorder-without-renumber assertion, the unchanged default, the orphan row, and the section-axis prefix the badge suppression depends on), expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-22 — **fix(questions): a lesson handle is no longer offered as a question category (D-#513).** D-#511 read every distinct `lesson_ref` as a category; the bank really does hold lesson handles there (`QP-MATH-C5-U03-L01-Q40` → `"L01"`), so MATH C5 grew a bogus `L01` chip and the whole group appeared for a subject with no categories. The read is now namespace-gated on `QCAT-`. Found by driving the running app, not by review. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, server + app tsc, server jest **3410/3410 (192 suites)** with 3 new, expo web export exit 0, and the live query re-checked (MATH C5 `["L01"]` → `[]`).
- 2026-08-22 — **feat(attendance): a per-group attendance breakdown — every Quran/Arabic group ranked side by side (AR-4, D-#514).** The ranking screen could show one group at a time or pool a whole track into a single list; neither answers "how is each group doing". Pooling is the worse of the two here, because groups mark on different schedules — on prod আম্মাপারা has held 28 days and হিফজ ১ বালক 4 — so one shared ranking compares records built on incomparable denominators. New **গ্রুপভিত্তিক** axis with a কুরআন / আরবি track chip: one card per group, each carrying its own `heldDays` in the header, its own ranked list with the class badge on every row, top 5 with a tap to expand, and a summary strip above (groups measured / students ranked / max held days / perfect attendance). Reuses the existing সময়কাল, তারিখ and সাজান controls, so week/month/cumulative and the class sort all work unchanged — the class sort reorders **within** each group. Backed by one new query, `subjectGroupAttendanceBreakdown`, which reads days, memberships, students and classes once each and partitions in memory rather than looping `rankStudents` per group (the D-#476 fan-out trap). A group that marked nothing still ships a card saying so, and is excluded from the summary counts. No permission, enum or wire-contract change. Gate: server + app tsc clean, vocab verifier PASS, **server jest 3421/3421 (192 suites)** with 6 new (per-group denominators, the idle-group card, the per-group floor, summary arithmetic, the class inside each group, and an empty track), expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-22 — **feat(teaching-notes): a (class × subject) library of teacher notes and how-to-answer guides, with an improvement-comment loop the Principal supervises (TN-1..TN-3, D-#519–#523).** Principal/Office upload a note per (class, subject, kind, seq) as markdown or PDF/Word; a new upload REPLACES the previous version (old row stamped `replacedAt`, retained) with a SERVER-assigned version number; the class's subject teachers read it and leave improvement comments; each comment is `OPEN` until the note's uploader or P/O marks it addressed, and the Principal gets a cross-subject outstanding list. Composed from proven parts — `StoredFile` + `/files/*` for bytes, the `EnglishDriveDoc` replace-by-identity shape for versioning, `resolveTeacherScopes` for reach, `createPrintRequest` for the office queue — so **no wire-contract change**: the subject axis is the existing `ROUTINE_SUBJECTS` enum and `kind` is app-native (the D-#46/#52 shape). **Three things are deliberately not the English Drive behaviour.** Visibility is (class × subject) PAIRS and walks the ROUTINE as well as the grants, because `Subject.code` is FOUNDATION_SUBJECTS and **ARABIC/QURAN have no `Subject` row** — a grant-only walk would have shipped an Arabic library no Arabic teacher could open, silently (D-#521). `classLevel` is the ROSTER axis so Nursery/KG are covered. And a comment anchors to the DOCUMENT IDENTITY, not the version row (D-#522): pinning it to the row would have deleted the whole thread the moment the improved file landed — exactly when someone wants to check whether it answered the feedback — so `versionSeen` renders "written on v2, current is v3" instead. **The upload also rejects mojibake** (D-#523): all four seed documents arrived as UTF-8 read as Latin-1, and an accepted one looks fine in the list until a teacher opens it. **TN-3 notifications included:** three app-native `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` + six template keys — note/version → the pair’s teachers (audience resolved from the ROUTINE, so Arabic/Quran are reached), comment → uploader + Principal, addressed → the teacher who raised it; nobody is ever notified of their own action. This edits `shared/vocab.ts` and the verifier list, which the unlanded `feat/exams` also touches (AGENTS §5) — built on the owner’s explicit instruction, every edit append-at-end so that rebase stays a both-sides-keep. Gate: **server jest 3473/3473 (194 suites)** with 58 new, server + app tsc clean, shared-vocab verifier PASS. Nothing driven at the running app yet.
- 2026-08-22 — **feat(questions): the bank filter is multi-select, and can filter by chapter (D-#524).** Topic tag and question type accept several values at once (`$in`), and a new chapter row filters on the artifact's own `address.number` — pick a chapter and every question TYPE in it comes back. `questionChapters` feeds the row and hides it where a slice has no chapters. The single-value args are kept so an APK that has not taken an OTA keeps working, and an empty selection widens rather than blanking. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, server + app tsc, server jest **3439/3439 (193 suites)** with 18 new, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-22 — **feat(questions): approve-with-condition, and chapter-wise review assignment (D-#525).** A reviewer can now approve a question WITH a stated condition: the question is held at `draft` (so it cannot be published), the condition text is mandatory, and clearing it sends the question back to the SAME reviewer for a fresh round rather than publishing it. The plan loop refuses the new verdict. The Principal can assign whole chapters to one reviewer in a single action, skipping published, already-reviewed and in-flight questions and reporting every skip. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, server + app tsc, server jest **3518/3518 (196 suites)** with 21 new, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-22 — **feat(hr): staff records can be created and edited in the app, and email logins can have a password regenerated (D-#526).** Adding one employee previously required a developer to run `import-staff.ts` against Atlas, which also meant they could never be given a login (a login is provisioned FROM a profile, D-#60) — and email accounts had no password recovery at all. New staff form (create + edit, PATCH semantics, pay excluded), an edit button on every staff row, and a regenerate-password action on email users that shows the new password once. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, server + app tsc, server jest **3534/3534 (197 suites)** with 16 new, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-24 — **fix(printing): a reprint's copy count is honoured — the Office no longer sees the ORIGINAL number (D-#294/#362).** Reported from live use: a teacher reprints from the history with a NEW copy count and the print queue keeps showing the old one. Cause: `reprintPrintRequest` cloned `copiesMode` **and** `copiesClassId` unconditionally, so a job that counted per class present came back as CLASS_PRESENT — and for such a job the typed number is dead weight by design (the queue renders `effectiveCopies` re-resolved from the USE day's attendance, and `markPrinted` finalizes `copies` from attendance again). The teacher typed 25, the Office saw 11. The reprint now takes an optional `copiesMode`: it still defaults to the original's mode, but a FIXED reprint drops `copiesClassId` so the typed count is what gets printed, and a CLASS_PRESENT reprint of a job that names no class is refused rather than silently downgraded (mode recorded in the reprint audit row). In the app, a row that counted per class present offers the same two chips as the original send-to-print card — picking "সংখ্যা লিখুন" gives a number field that is honoured end to end, keeping "শ্রেণির উপস্থিত অনুযায়ী" hides the field and says the count comes from that day's attendance instead of showing a number that does nothing. A FIXED row is untouched, which is why the bug read as intermittent. Gate: server jest **3555/3555 (199 suites)** with 3 new, server + app tsc clean, expo web export exit 0.
- 2026-08-23 — **feat(exams): the exam syllabus, its mark distribution and its question types become an app record (D-#530–#532; `docs/prd-exam-syllabus.md`, slices SY-1..SY-6).** The Annual Exam Syllabus is a photocopied Word table today; it now has a home. Office writes one syllabus per (exam × class × subject) — prose, a numbered মানবন্টন and the question types; the **subject teacher signs it off** (derived from the ROUTINE, the only source that reaches ARABIC/QURAN); the **Principal publishes**, which is the single guardian-visible predicate. Teachers and guardians read the same renderer the PDF uses, as a grid of subject-name buttons with the syllabus and mark distribution behind one tap. `Σ marks = 100` is one universal guard in every class, a CT/আদব row is tagged as the report-card component it actually is, and written/oral is derived rather than typed twice. `QUESTION_TYPES` is deliberately untouched (D-#530). Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3605/3605 (200 suites)** with 64 new, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-23 — **feat(exams): the three gaps left open in the syllabus slices are closed.** A self-audit against `docs/prd-exam-syllabus.md` found three things named in the slice bodies but never built. (1) **`ExamClassNote` had no write path** — the per-class question-type footer could only be seeded from a script; it now has a mutation and its own screen, deliberately NOT gated on the syllabus status machine because it states exam FORMAT rather than what a teacher must cover, and would otherwise become un-editable the moment one subject advanced. (2) **The drawer badge** promised by SY-5 now exists, fed by a lightweight `mySyllabusApprovalCount` rather than the full rows (the drawer polls it every 60s), keyed on the LEAF because the three syllabus leaves share one route. (3) **The Principal's class × subject matrix** replaced the filtered list — five states, each with a Bangla glyph as well as a colour (UI guidelines §0), fed by one `examSyllabusBoard` query rather than seven per-class calls. Also: `createExam` moved into a service so the "a second exam for the same year+term is refused" acceptance box could actually be executed rather than merely coded. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3621/3621 (201 suites)** with 16 new, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-24 — **docs(exams): manual test guide for the exam-syllabus module.** `docs/test-plan-exam-syllabus.md` — 111 checks across the four roles, in the house test-plan shape (it is both the script and the bug report). Grounded in the live code rather than the PRD's intentions, so it opens with what is **not** built and must not be logged as a bug: there is no create-exam screen (step 1.1 does it over GraphQL), there are no notifications (the drawer badge is the only signal), there is no bulk publish, and no 2026 content is loaded. It also names the six deliberate gotchas a tester would otherwise file — Office holding `exam:manage` and still being refused publish, sign-off coming from the routine rather than a permission, a subject teacher who is not the *named* approver being refused, an edit silently re-opening an approved row, an unpublished subject showing as a dimmed button rather than vanishing, and Σ = 100 holding in every class. §9 spot-checks the three shared surfaces the feature touched (teaching-notes' moved encoding guard, the Print/Class-Test drawer badges after `tintedBadgesFor` changed signature, the Reports deep links).

- 2026-08-24 — **feat(exams): the whole syllabus flow is reachable from the UI (SY-7).** Exam creation had no screen at all — `createExam` was a mutation only, so step one of the module required a developer with a GraphQL client, which is exactly the person the school does not have. Adds **পরীক্ষা ব্যবস্থাপনা** (`exam:manage`): create an exam, and edit its name/date window through a new `updateExam` PATCH. Year and term are pickers on create and **plain text on edit** — together they are the row's identity and its unique index, and every syllabus hangs off the exam id, so moving one would silently re-home all of them. The term picker offers only terms the chosen year does not already have, which turns the duplicate refusal into something you can see coming. Two bugs found while auditing and fixed in the same pass: the per-subject **exam date was labelled "পরীক্ষা"** and typed as free text against a format hint (now the same date picker every other date uses), and the Principal's card **offered প্রকাশ করুন on every cell of the matrix** including rows still with Office or the teacher — a button whose only possible outcome was the server's refusal; actions are now gated per stage, and the mark-distribution toggle no longer shows the same label open and closed. `docs/test-plan-exam-syllabus.md` §1.1 is rewritten from GraphQL to the screen. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3639/3639 (202 suites)** with 7 new, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-24 — **fix(exams): the syllabus coverage board was empty for the people who write it.** Found on prod within minutes of promoting, by opening the screen. `classSyllabus` only returned subjects that ALREADY had an `ExamSyllabus` row, and generated placeholders for teachers only — so Office and the Principal got "০ এর মধ্যে ০ টি বিষয়" with nothing to tap and **no way to create the first syllabus**. Unwritable by construction; the whole module was unusable. Admin callers now get a placeholder for every subject the **routine** says the class sits (section slots for that class plus cross-grade subjectgroup slots — the only path that reaches ARABIC and QURAN, which have no `Subject` row), falling back to the full subject list when a class has no routine yet so it can never be a dead end. Two smaller things from the same screenshot: the missing-class-note banner rendered the **label** `শ্রেণি ভিত্তিক নোট` where a sentence belongs (now `syClassNoteMissing`), and a class with no subjects rendered an **empty white Card** instead of an empty state. **Why the tests missed it:** every existing test mocked the row list and handed back rows, so none of them ever saw the empty database the school actually starts from — six new tests pin that state, including the no-routine fallback and a check that the admin branch does not widen a teacher's board. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3648/3648 (202 suites)** with 6 new, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-24 — **fix(exams): a syllabus could not be submitted the first time it was written.** Found on prod by pressing the button. Submit was gated on `!stored?.id`, and `stored` is the SAVED row — a subject being written for the first time is a placeholder with a null id, so the primary action was permanently dead on exactly the case it exists for: a fresh syllabus, balanced at ১০০, approver named from the routine, and a grey button explaining nothing. `onSubmit` was broken the same way underneath: it saved and then read `stored?.id` out of its own render closure, which is still the pre-save value, so even an enabled press would have refetched and silently returned. `onSave` now RETURNS the saved id and `onSubmit` submits with it, so a first write saves-and-sends in one press; the gate depends only on what the caller can act on (rows balance, routine names an approver) and **every disabled state now says why** — the missing-approver case previously had no message at all. Six new tests read the screen source statically, because this was a well-typed boolean that was simply wrong: `tsc` and `expo export` both passed it. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3654/3654 (203 suites)** with 6 new, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-24 — **fix(trackers,app): the class-test pass mark can be corrected while results are still DRAFT, and a refused save now says why (D-#536).** From live prod: a Class-2 English test with 14 DRAFT results refused a pass-mark correction 17→15, and the screen showed nothing — the owner retried the same save seven times while the server answered clearly on every attempt. `updateClassTestDetails` no longer refuses on "any result exists"; it splits the fields by what each actually re-grades — `totalMarks` still refused (the denominator of every percentage, D-#121), `examDate` still refused (the D-#120 deadline anchor), `passMark` allowed while every result is DRAFT and refused once any is SUBMITTED or RELEASED (D-#277: no un-notified guardian-visible flip). pass/fail is derived, never stored (D-#85), so nothing is re-graded in the data. A no-op re-save no longer trips a guard. App: the server’s reason renders beside the Save button (the screen’s only Notice lived in the CANCELLED early-return branch, so the error was set in state and never shown) and is cleared when the form reopens. Gate: server + app tsc clean, vocab verifier PASS, server jest class-test sweep **177/177 (7 suites)** with 4 new.
- 2026-08-24 — **fix(questions): the reviewer queue is paginated, and a verdict no longer re-pulls it.** Reported as "the app hangs" by one teacher while another's account was fine — the classic shape of an unbounded per-user read. `listMyQuestionReviews` had no limit, and on prod that reviewer held **2,742 assigned rounds**: 2,743 rounds joined to 2,743 content artifacts and serialised with every question's full payload — measured at **1.77 MB in one response, 755 ms of database time** — then 2,742 cards rendered at once. It froze rather than errored, which is why no log line named it. `myQuestionReviews` now takes `limit`/`offset` (default 50, capped 200, stable `_id` tiebreak so a page boundary cannot repeat or skip a row), with a new `myQuestionReviewCount` for the pager's denominator; the screen accumulates pages behind a আরও দেখুন control and shows N / total, because 50 rows out of 2,742 otherwise reads as "nearly done". **Approve/reject was slow for the same reason:** every verdict ended in `refetch(network-only)`, re-downloading the entire queue, so the cost of deciding one question scaled with how much work the reviewer had left — decided rows are now dropped from the local list instead. An old APK that sends neither argument degrades to the first 50 rather than to the hang. Also: the mark-distribution rows no longer repeat the item-type under each row (owner ask — it printed "ছোট প্রশ্ন" under most of fifteen rows; the types are already listed once as chips). Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3662/3662 (204 suites)** with 8 new, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-24 — **fix(questions): the queue pager was invisible because it depended on a second query.** Reported as "can't see any pagination button" straight after the pagination shipped. Ruled out the obvious causes first — the deployed bundle really did contain the new code, and `index.html` is `no-cache`, so it was not a stale client. The flaw was mine: `hasMore = rows.length < total`, which couples the pager's EXISTENCE to `myQuestionReviewCount`. When that count is slow, refused or errored, `total` falls back to `0`, `hasMore` goes false, and `LoadOlder` renders its **exhausted** state — 50 rows out of 2,742 with no way to reach the rest, and nothing to distinguish that from genuinely having reached the end. `hasMore` is now decided by the **last page's size**: a full page is self-contained evidence that more may exist, a short page is the end. The count is demoted to the "N / M" caption and the caption degrades to a plain row count when it is unavailable. Also aligned `myQuestionReviewCount` to the house `t.field({ type: "Int" })` shape the other count fields use. Four new tests read the screen source to pin that `hasMore` never mentions `total`, that the client page size matches the server default, that a reload re-arms the pager, and that a verdict still does not re-pull the queue. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3670/3670**, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-24 — **fix(questions): loading more no longer throws the reader to the top of the queue.** `RefreshControl` was wired to `fetching`, which is true for a *load more* as well as for a pull-to-refresh — and a RefreshControl turning on drags the list back to the top. Tapping আরও দেখুন therefore jumped to the first card with nothing to show whether rows had been appended. `refreshing` is now its own flag, raised only by `reload()` and cleared when a page lands. The load-more control also carries the progress (`আরও দেখুন (৫০ / ২৭৪২)`), because the N / M caption sits at the TOP and a reader who has just paged is at the bottom and cannot see it. Four more static tests. Gate: verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3674/3674**, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-24 — **fix(trackers): a blank academic-year id on the assignment week is a business denial, not a reported CastError.** From GlitchTip (`scdhub-server`, 2 events 2026-08-13): `CastError: Cast to ObjectId failed for value "" at path "academicYearId" for model "AssignmentSchedule"`. `assignmentExpectedWeek` takes a GraphQL `String!` — non-null but still satisfied by `""` — so a client with no academic year resolved sent one through to `findOne({ academicYearId: "" })`. Because `CastError` keeps its own `constructor.name` it is NOT covered by the D-#387 expected-error rules, so a client-side blank paged the maintainer as a server fault. `expectedItemsForWeek` now rejects a blank/malformed id with a plain `Error` (this codebase’s business-denial convention — unreported, and safe to show the user per D-#259) BEFORE Mongoose can cast it. The guard sits in the service, not the resolver, because all three callers funnel through it and the other two avoid this today only by luck of where their ids come from. Gate: server tsc clean, server jest **3629/3629 (201 suites)** with 4 new.

- 2026-08-25 — **fix(app): patch react-native-screens so the app stops hard-crashing on every device below Android 15.** GlitchTip `scdhub-app`, FATAL, 26 events / 4 users on `com.scd.hub@1.1.3+6` (Galaxy Note 10+ SM-N975F, A10 SM-A105F, Huawei CTR-AL20): `NoSuchMethodError: No interface method removeLast()Ljava/lang/Object; in class Ljava/util/List;`, innermost frame `com.swmansion.rnscreens.ScreenStack.obtainDrawingOp` line 319 — matching `ScreenStack.kt:319` exactly. `List.removeLast()` is a Java 21 SequencedCollection method that first appears in API 35’s `android.jar`; because `app/android/gradle.properties` carries a LOCAL convenience override `android.compileSdkVersion=35` (added because this laptop lacks the android-34 platform), Kotlin bound the interface MEMBER instead of the stdlib extension, and the method does not exist at runtime below Android 15. Fixed via patch-package (already wired through `postinstall`, precedent `fontkit+2.0.4`): `removeLast()` → `removeAt(size - 1)`, which compiles to `List.remove(int)` and exists on every level. Swept the other native modules — react-native-screens is the ONLY one calling removeFirst/removeLast. NOTE: this needs a NEW APK BUILD to reach users; merging alone changes nothing for them, and the compileSdk=35 override remains the underlying trap for any future dependency.

- 2026-08-25 — **fix(app): a null field in a partial GraphQL response no longer crashes the screen.** GlitchTip `scdhub-app`, 14 events still arriving: `React ErrorBoundary TypeError: Cannot read property ’length’ of null`. Breadcrumbs showed the mechanism — the app resumes to foreground, `GET version.json` and `POST /graphql` both return `status_code: 0` (no connectivity), `Network request failed` fires, and the ErrorBoundary trips; so this and the `TypeError: Network request failed` issue are ONE incident, not two. The code-side cause is a bug class: five sites guarded the query `data` object but NOT the nested field, so a partial response (`data` present, one field `null` — exactly what a scope-auth rejection returns, and the prod logs are full of `ForbiddenError: Not authorized to resolve Query.me / myPermissions / myTemplates`) crashed on `.length`. All five hardened with `?? []`, and in every case the CONSUMING branch too — `GroupRoutineScreen` passed `routineSlots` into `<SlotList>` BEFORE the length check, so guarding the check alone would have kept crashing. The sweep now returns zero unguarded sites. Gate: app tsc clean.

- 2026-08-25 — **chore(release): v1.1.4 (versionCode 7).** Bumped so the two app-side crash fixes can actually reach phones: the react-native-screens `removeLast()` patch (FATAL on every device below Android 15 — 26 events, 4 users, all still on `1.1.3+6`) and the null-field ErrorBoundary guard. Neither ships without a new APK, and `UpdateGate` walls on `versionCode`, so leaving it at 6 would have published a build no phone would take. `runtimeVersion` stays `1.1.0` (OTA channel, unchanged — this is a full APK replacement, not an OTA).

- 2026-08-25 — **fix(exams): saving a syllabus a second time was rejected outright.** `Variable "$marks" got invalid value { __typename: "SyllabusMarkRow", … }; Field "__typename" is not defined by type "SyllabusMarkRowInput"`. urql's `cacheExchange` stamps `__typename` onto every object it returns; the editor hydrated its rows with `{ ...m }` and sent them back with `{ ...r }`, so the marker travelled from the query result into local state and out again as mutation input — and GraphQL rejects the WHOLE mutation when an input object carries a field its type does not define. The **first** save of a new subject always worked, because those rows come from `emptyRow()` and never touch the cache; only a save-reopen-save cycle failed, which is exactly why it survived the walkthrough — every subject was written once. Both the hydrate and the save path now go through a `toDraft` helper that rebuilds a row field by field, so no future cache metadata can ride along either. Four new tests read the screen source, because this is invisible to `tsc`: `__typename` is a perfectly legal extra property on a spread. Gate: verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3682/3682**, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-25 — **fix(android): drop the local `compileSdkVersion=35` override — it was the root cause of the fatal Android<15 crash AND was blocking the build that fixes it.** The line was added as a convenience because this laptop lacked the android-34 platform. It caused two separate failures. (1) **Runtime, in production:** API 35’s `android.jar` is the first to declare `java.util.List.removeLast()` (Java 21 SequencedCollection), so Kotlin bound the interface MEMBER in `react-native-screens` instead of its stdlib extension; that method does not exist below Android 15, giving `NoSuchMethodError` — FATAL, 26 events across 4 users. (2) **Build:** API 35 marks `PackageInfo.requestedPermissions` `@Nullable`, which does not compile in `expo-modules-core` 1.12.26 (`PermissionsService.kt:166`), so the release build failed outright. Installed `platforms;android-34` (build-tools 34.0.0 was already present) and removed both overrides, so the build now uses the `build.gradle` defaults — 34 / 34.0.0 — which is what Expo SDK 51 expects. Verified by the shipped artifact: `aapt2 dump badging` reports `compileSdkVersion=34`. With 34 restored the `removeLast()` binding cannot occur at all, so the react-native-screens patch becomes belt-and-braces rather than load-bearing — kept deliberately, to protect whoever raises compileSdk next. A comment recording both failure modes now sits where the override was.

- 2026-08-25 — **fix(routine): the teacher class-load report no longer shows a superseded routine slot alongside its replacement.** Owner report from prod: Shah Mahfuj Ahmed’s Sunday showed প্রথম শ্রেণি · বাংলা at BOTH P5 and P6, while the routine grid correctly showed P6 as Husne ara Rahman Fida’s গণিত. Cause: `teacherClassLoad` queries every slot version whose `[effectiveFrom, effectiveTo]` window OVERLAPS the month — correct for the calendar-accurate `monthTotal`, wrong for the weekly view, which fed `perWeekday`, `weekTotal` and the drill-down grid straight from that union. After the 2026-08-24 change moving his বাংলা P6 → P5, both versions matched August. The weekly view is now a SNAPSHOT of the timetable in force at a reference date (today, clamped into the month: current → today, past → month end, future → month start), so it agrees with the routine screen. Verified against prod data: `weekTotal` 27 → 25, and Sunday now reads P1 P2 P3 P5 P7 — P6 gone. `monthTotal` is unchanged and was always right. **Also corrected a doc/test error rather than shipping it:** the file header claimed a half-open `[effectiveFrom, effectiveTo)` window, and a first cut of this fix “tightened” the boundary to match — but `RoutineSlotService` closes a superseded slot with `endOfDayBefore(changeFrom)`, so the window is CLOSED and the replacement starts 00:00 the next day; the two never overlap and no changeover day was ever double-counted. The header now describes the real convention and the new test uses the timestamps the writer actually stores instead of an invented overlap.

- 2026-08-25 — **feat(questions): reviewer progress — who was given what, and how they ruled (QR-5, D-#537).** The owner asked what a named reviewer had done with a class they were assigned; nothing in the app answered it. The assign screen shows an `Assigned to` badge with no reviewer filter, no counts, and no published questions (it filters `reviewStatus ≠ gold`, so it is not a record of what was handed over); the publish screen mixes every reviewer and every class into two tabs. New: `questionReviewerProgress` — one row per reviewer (assigned / pending / approved / with-condition / rejected / closed-undecided / decided), narrowable by class and subject, ordered by who still owes work — plus `questionReviewerRounds` + `questionReviewerRoundCount` for the drill-down behind each counter, and a two-screen app flow (progress cards → bucket tabs → the existing round thread). **The counts are bucketed by VERDICT, not by round status, and that is the whole design:** `publishQuestion` calls `supersedeOpenRounds`, so a published question's round goes `submitted → superseded` with its verdict untouched — a status-based tally would have emptied a reviewer's approved column as the Principal published their work. `CANCELLED` is derived by subtraction so the buckets always add back up to `assigned`. Kept as its own query rather than filters on `questionReviewInbox`, which stays pinned to `status: submitted` because "the publish queue" is a different question. The drill-down is paginated from birth (50/page, capped 200) — it is the same rows, carrying the same `payloadJson`, as the unbounded read that froze a reviewer's phone at 2,742 rounds on 2026-08-24. **Companion fix in the same slice:** `APPROVE_WITH_CONDITION` had no Principal-side UI at all — the publish screen's tab type was `"APPROVE" | "CHANGES_REQUESTED"`, so the verdict was invisible, and `clearQuestionCondition` had existed since `fa2f418` with no caller anywhere in the app; a conditional approval simply stalled where nobody could see it. It now has a tab, its condition text, and a clear action whose hint says what clearing does (returns the question to the same reviewer — it does not publish). Gate: verifier PASS, shared + server + app `tsc` clean, server jest **3699/3699 (206 suites)** with **17 new**, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-25 — **fix(app): the last three GlitchTip app issues — a re-entrant file open, and "No Content" reported as a fault.** (1) **`ExponentFileSystem.moveAsync` rejected** (1 event): `openStoredFileAndroid` downloaded to a temp path FIXED per file (`scdhub-tmp-${fileId}`), so two overlapping opens of the same attachment wrote to the same path — the first `moveAsync` renamed it away and the second failed. The temp path now carries a per-call suffix. (2) **`ExpoIntentLauncher.startActivity` rejected** (6 events): Android permits one launched activity at a time, so a tap landing on top of an already-open viewer threw "already started". That is user-recoverable, not a defect: it is now raised as a `FileUploadError`, which reaches every caller’s existing catch and shows a notice instead of a crash report. Both share one cause, so `openStoredFile` also gained a re-entrancy guard keyed by file id — placed at the shared choke point because `useFileOpen` (BUG-014) only guards re-entry WITHIN one component, and `ClassNoteAttachments` / `DeliverAssignmentScreen` call through directly. (3) **`Error: No Content`** (7 events): urql throws this when a response carries no body — a request cut short by backgrounding, unmount or a dropped connection. The app already surfaces and retries it, so it is now filtered at the reporting boundary, the same rule the server applies in `sentry.ts` (D-#387). `TypeError: Network request failed` is deliberately NOT filtered — it is the signal that identified the offline-resume incident. Gate: app tsc clean, expo web export exit 0 (real exit code, not `tail`’s).

- 2026-08-25 — **docs(questions): manual test guide for reviewer progress (QR-5).** `docs/test-plan-qr5-reviewer-progress.md` — eight cases in the house test-plan shape (both the script and the bug report). Opens with the two properties that look like bugs if you do not know them: counters count **rounds, not distinct questions** (a re-assign or a cleared condition is a second piece of work), and a decision **keeps counting after its question is published**. T4 is the load-bearing case — note the approved count, publish one of that reviewer's approvals, confirm the count did **not** drop; if it dropped, the counters have been rewired onto round status and every reviewer's record now empties as the Principal works through it. T7 covers the verdict that had no UI at all until this release, and says so plainly: a non-zero **শর্তসাপেক্ষ** count means those questions have been stalled since the verdict was first used, and clearing one sends it **back to the reviewer** rather than publishing it — so the reviewer's `অপেক্ষমাণ` and `বরাদ্দ` both rise by one, which is correct and not a miscount. T8 pins the three permission shapes, including the reviewer-only teacher whose account shape white-screened the app in `791e5fe`. Also names what is deliberately absent: no phone build yet (web only until a new APK), no `published` count in the rollup, no reviewer nudge.

- 2026-08-25 — **chore(release): v1.1.5 (versionCode 8).** Ships the three app-side fixes that landed AFTER v1.1.4 was built and so reached no phone: the re-entrant file open (per-call temp path + an in-flight guard on `openStoredFile`, fixing both `ExponentFileSystem.moveAsync` and `ExpoIntentLauncher.startActivity` rejections) and the urql `No Content` reporting filter. v1.1.4 was built from `d8f8bca`, before those merged — resolving them in GlitchTip without this build would have claimed a fix users could not have. First build on the restored `compileSdk 34` (D-#: the local 35 override was removed), so the react-native-screens `removeLast()` patch is now belt-and-braces rather than load-bearing. `runtimeVersion` stays `1.1.0` — full APK replacement, not an OTA.

- 2026-08-25 — **feat(questions): publish filters, publish-all, and the answer the Principal was judging blind (QR-6, D-#538).** Owner, testing QR-5 on prod: add a publish-all and filters, and "conditional parts are not clear to me — what are the original options and what message sent from reviewer". The last one turned out to be a defect, not a question. **The condition text IS the reviewer's whole message** (mandatory free text on `APPROVE_WITH_CONDITION`, stored as the round's `feedback`), but **the options and answer key were never rendered** — `payloadJson` has been fetched by these screens since QR-4 and thrown away, so "শর্ত: বের হওয়া বা বাহির হওয়া" sat next to a question whose current answer key was invisible. `D-#530` fixed exactly this for the REVIEWER after she reported approving MCQs blind; nobody checked one screen over. The shared `AnswerCarrier` is now on all three Principal surfaces — publish, the QR-5 drill-down and the thread — and the condition/rejection lines are attributed to the reviewer by name, since they are her words and not a system label. No server change for that half. **Filters** (subject / class / chapter / question type / search) land on `questionReviewInbox`, with `questionReviewInboxCount` beside it; the three round-level axes stay a plain `find` because they are denormalised onto the round, and only `questionType`/`search` add a `$lookup`. The inbox is also **paginated at last** — it was the module's final unbounded read, the same shape as the one that froze a reviewer's phone at 2,742 rounds, and adding filters without a ceiling would have encouraged bigger result sets rather than smaller. **Publish-all** (`publishQuestionsMatching`) re-derives the set server-side from the SAME filter builder the list and count use, rather than trusting ids from a screen that holds only the pages it has scrolled — because every write to `reviewStatus` runs one way (`draft → reviewed → gold`, nothing demotes), so a confirmation quoting 47 while a different set publishes is unrecoverable. APPROVE-only (a rejected question needs its own override reason, D-#525), capped per call with `remaining` returned so truncation is visible, and — per the owner's ruling — allowed with no filter at all, the count in the confirmation being the warning. Gate: verifier PASS, shared + server + app `tsc` clean, server jest **3719/3719 (207 suites)** with **17 new**, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-25 — **fix(release): `version.json` points at the VERSIONED apk, not the mutable `scd-hub-latest.apk` — the edge was serving the previous release for 4h after every publish.** Caught while verifying the v1.1.5 publish: the file on the VM was 1.1.5 (78,295,482 bytes) but `https://scdhub.shafayet.me/downloads/scd-hub-latest.apk` returned **78,289,663** — v1.1.4 — with `cf-cache-status: HIT`, `Age: 3447`, `Cache-Control: max-age=14400` and `last-modified` equal to the 1.1.4 publish time. `scd-hub-latest.apk` is ONE url whose bytes change every release, so Cloudflare’s default 4-hour cache kept serving the old binary while `version.json` (deliberately `no-store`, per the Caddyfile comment) already advertised the new `versionCode` — phones were told “update to N” and handed N-1. The Caddyfile author had foreseen exactly this for the manifest and protected it; the apk itself was missed. `apkUrl` now names `scd-hub-${versionName}-c${versionCode}.apk`, which is never reused, so caching it is not merely safe but desirable. The upload order already created that file before swapping the manifest, so the pointer can never precede the bytes. The live `version.json` was repointed by hand at once — no Cloudflare purge needed, because the versioned url was never stale. STILL OPEN: the manual first-install link on the login screen still points at `scd-hub-latest.apk`, so a brand-new install can fetch a stale apk for up to 4h (it self-updates afterwards); fixing that needs either a Caddy `Cache-Control` header on that one path or a versioned link.

- 2026-08-25 — **feat(hr): the Staff Hub — one screen for a staff member's whole lifecycle, plus letters, a shared 20-day leave pool, the probation held-debt ledger and the 3-lates rule (SH-1..SH-7, D-#539–#545).** Owner ask, with the EximusEdu teacher profile as the reference: *"in the eximus the starting to ending i can see in a single place. but in our app i have to jump in 10 different places."* Pre-flight found the engine already shipped — `StaffProfile`, `provisionStaffLogin`, leave, `TeacherAttendanceDay` (punch in/out/shift), payroll with an unwired `latenessDeduction` slot, offboarding, and the pdfkit + Noto-Bengali renderer — and that the real gap was a **surface** (there was no staff detail screen anywhere in the app) plus four genuinely new rules. **SH-1** `StaffLetter`: a letter is a FROZEN RECORD, its `snapshot` built once at issue and the PDF (`GET /pdf/staff-letter/:id`, `staff:manage`) rendering from it alone, so re-printing after a profile edit still produces the page the person signed; never edited, only voided (kept, marked VOID on its face, still renderable) with a never-reused per-year ref no. The source `.docx`'s two contradictions are resolved rather than reproduced — clause 1 (salary) and clause 2 (honorary) are mutually exclusive, and clause 6 names the real designation instead of the template's stray "your duties as a principal"; issuing REFUSES with no designation or a paid letter with no salary. **SH-2** `confirmationDate` + `confirmStaffEmployment`, an event with a settlement rather than a field edit. **SH-3** the shared 20-day pool (`HrPolicy`, read-time defaults, no seed write against the live Atlas) and the `ProbationLeaveDebt` ledger — probation leave is held, not deducted, and settles against the new pool at confirmation or against the final settlement at exit. **SH-4** `LatenessCharge`: 3 lates = 1 day, leave pool first then salary, leftovers forgiven monthly, frozen at payroll lock, and shipping **OFF** so no existing payslip figure changes on deploy. **SH-5/6/7** the per-tab reads, `StaffHubScreen` (প্রোফাইল · উপস্থিতি · ছুটি · বেতন · কাগজপত্র), the 4-step join wizard replacing the three unconnected stops joining used to take, and the own-row leave picture on `MyRecordScreen`. No new permission — every gate is one that already existed. Gate: vocab verifier PASS (15 new checks), shared + server + app `tsc` clean, server jest **3760/3760 (208 suites)** with **41 new**, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-25 — **fix(hr): probation-held leave must not be docked by payroll as well as collected by the ledger (D-#546).** Found while re-reading the payroll path immediately before promoting SH-1..SH-7, and it inverted the owner's own rule: D-#540 records probation leave as unpaid and settles it later, but `unpaidLeaveDaysByStaff` deducts day-rate × `unpaidDays` for every approved leave in the month, and the probation path sets `unpaidDays = days`. So the absence would have been deducted from that month's salary AND collected a second time at confirmation or exit — the same days charged twice, to the one person the rule exists to protect. A `probationHeld` flag on `StaffLeaveApplication` (absent reads as false, no migration) marks the held rows; payroll filters them out; re-approving after confirmation clears both the flag and the ledger row so the days become payable exactly once. Also ships `server/scripts/backfill-staff-confirmation-date.ts` (dry-run by default) — without it every already-confirmed staff member reads as still-on-probation the moment SH-3 lands, because `confirmationDate` is a new field. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, server tsc clean, full server jest green.

- 2026-08-25 — **feat(hr): an HR-policy screen, and the own-row attendance + payslips the app never actually queried (SH-8, D-#547).** Two gaps found reviewing the shipped Staff Hub against its own PRD. **The policy screen** (`payroll:manage`, reached from the HR hub) exposes what SH-3/SH-4 shipped with no surface: the shared annual leave pool, the probation-hold switch, the lateness rule and its 3-days threshold, and the letter signatory/hours/ref-prefix. Until now the lateness rule — a feature the owner asked for by name — could only be enabled by hand-writing a mutation. Each switch states its blast radius on the screen: lateness applies from the next payroll preparation and never restates a locked payslip, changing the pool recomputes every balance, and letter edits reach NEW letters only because an issued letter's snapshot froze its signatory (D-#542). **The own-row reads**: `myStaffAttendance` (AT-1) and `myPayslips` (PR-2, locked runs only) have existed on the server for months with no app operation calling them, so a teacher could not see their own punch times or their own payslip anywhere — `MyRecordScreen` now shows both, and the stale header comment claiming they were "closed elsewhere" is corrected. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc clean, GraphQL documents validate, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-25 — **perf(questions): a 244-question publish stops taking minutes, and stops reporting a phantom remainder (D-#549).** Owner published 244 in one action: "it took lots of time" and "1 issue remain". Neither was the database being slow. **The cost was round trips.** `publishQuestionBulk` looped `publishQuestion`, and each item cost about six sequential Atlas calls — `findById`, `save`, the open-round read, an `updateOne` per open round, an audit insert per round, then the publish audit. 244 questions ≈ **1,500 sequential network hops**, entirely latency-bound. It now does the identical writes and the identical audit rows in a fixed handful of queries whatever the batch size: one read, one `updateMany`, one round read, one `updateMany`, one `writeAuditMany`. Refusals are unchanged message for message, so a question fails the same way whichever door it came through, and the single-question path (which carries the override reason) is untouched. **`remaining` was arithmetically incapable of being right:** the total counted ROUNDS while `okCount` counted ARTIFACTS, so two rounds on one question left a phantom 1 — almost certainly what the owner saw — and any legitimate failure counted as outstanding forever, so pressing again could never clear it. Publishing supersedes the rounds it publishes, so re-asking the same filter afterwards is correct by construction; the pre-count is gone. New `writeAuditMany` is the batched twin of `writeAudit` — same fields, same stamping, same never-throws contract, `ordered: false` so one rejected row cannot discard a batch of an append-only log. Gate: verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3722/3722 (207 suites)** with 3 new pinning the query-count ceiling, the per-question audit rows, and the re-count.

- 2026-08-26 — **feat(questions): a question can finally be corrected, or retired (QR-8, D-#548).** The bank had no write path — questions arrived by envelope import and could only be reviewed and published — so the reviewer condition D-#538 had just made visible was a message nobody could act on. Principal and Office now hold `question:manage`: correct the text, marks, options, true/false answer, accepted blanks or model note IN PLACE (never the subject/class/chapter/type address), or RETIRE the question softly so every `AssessmentSet` already holding its `artifactId`/`qid` keeps resolving. Retired questions drop out of the bank, the assign picker and the counts; retirement is reversible; a GOLD question may be edited and is audited with `wasPublished: true`. Three audit kinds carry the before AND after values. App: a `QuestionEditSheet` on the bank preview and the publish queue. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3779/3779 (209 suites)** with 15 new, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-26 — **feat(questions): a question can be marked বুরুত্বপূর্ণ / important (QR-9, D-#550).** Normal is the usual state; the mark is raised by a reviewer on a question in her own queue, or by Principal and Office from the bank at any time. It shows as a GOLD tag on the reviewer queue, the bank list, the question preview and the publish queue, and is separately filterable — `questions(important: true)`, with a বুরুত্বপূর্ণ chip in the filter sheet and an active-filter chip on the bar. Visible to EVERYONE who can see the question, teachers included, because the mark is a signal for whoever assembles a set. Marking is idempotent (a no-op writes nothing and logs nothing), audited both ways with `viaReviewQueue` separating a reviewer’s flag from a desk one, and SURVIVES a re-import of the same qid — unlike an in-place edit, which the upload overwrites. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3794/3794 (210 suites)** with 16 new, expo web export exit 0.
- 2026-08-25 — **feat(trackers): GC-1 — vocabulary, the `GuardianWorkClaim` model and the notification refs for the guardian “done at home” declaration (D-#551..#554/#557).** Three app-native enums + four notification kinds (no envelope twin, D-#46/#52); the claim row with the one-open-claim PARTIAL-UNIQUE index and the stored `actionDateKey`; `workClaimId` added to BOTH the `Notification` interface and `RefsSchema` (a ref in only one is silently stripped on write). Vocab verifier PASS, server tsc clean.

- 2026-08-25 — **feat(trackers): GC-2..GC-5 server — the guardian claim service, the auto-resolve hook, chase suppression, the staff resolvers and the 11:30/13:00 rungs (D-#551..#554/#557).** The claim auto-closes on `transitionRecord`s SUBMITTED edge (one place per tracker, so no future submit path can bypass it); an open claim mutes the family-facing chase push; the Office/Principal queue is visible from the instant a claim is filed while notification stays laddered; both rungs ride the existing 60s ticker. Server tsc clean, 43 new tests green.

- 2026-08-25 — **feat(trackers): RL-1/RL-2 server — the return-from-leave card and its attendance-fired push (D-#555/#556).** `myDay.returningStudents` is derived on every load from two sources (attendance-confirmed + leave-register-expected) with no stored row; items split into পুনরায় দিতে হবে (`ABSENT_REDELIVER`) and জমা নিতে হবে (`DUE`/`CHASE`), never mixed. The push fires from the attendance mark/amend seam on the CONFIRMED source only, to the class teacher only. Server tsc clean, 13 new tests green.

- 2026-08-25 — **feat(app): GC-3/GC-4/GC-5 + RL-1 screens — the guardian button, the teacher card and reject sheet, the Office queue and the return-from-leave card (D-#551..#557).** One `WorkClaimBlock` serves both guardian record screens (the two record models are symmetric, so a family meets one affordance); `canClaim` is server-computed so no screen re-implements the D-#553 rule; the Office queue sorts by CHECKPOINT rather than age, because the same-day ladder made "how many days old" the wrong question. App tsc clean, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-26 — **fix(hr): twelve defects from driving the Staff Hub in production (SH-9, D-#551–#556).** The feature shipped green — 3764 tests, typecheck, export — and an hour of clicking through it found twelve things no test asserted. **Four mattered.** The hub rendered the navigation parameter it was handed rather than live data, so a confirmation that fully succeeded still showed শিক্ষানবিশ with the button inviting a second press; it now reads `staffProfile(id)` and refetches on focus. A paid letter could promise a salary the record did not carry — the override lived only in the frozen snapshot — so it now writes back, audited as a pay change `via: "letter"`. `setStaffPay` treated a NaN-turned-null salary as "leave unchanged" and saved the payment method alone, which is how that mismatch arose; all three salary fields now parse digits-only and the server rejects non-finite. And `LetterError` was never registered in `EXPOSED_DOMAIN_ERRORS`, so its clear refusal reached the operator as "Unexpected error." — D-#536 repeating in code written days after it. **The rest:** a payment method is now collected with its account number (owner's catch — the disbursement file carries exactly that); a probationer's pool reads "স্থায়ী হলে পাবেন" instead of "ছুটি বাকি", which said the opposite of the rule; salary can be set from the বেতন tab instead of the far side of the app; the lateness rule has a card of its own rather than being invisible until someone is late; মূল্যায়ন and প্রস্থান tabs and a পাসওয়ার্ড রিসেট action close the gap between the hub and its wireframe; the wizard suggests the next staff id, localises the payment chips and shows the role the login was given; and the স্থায়ীকরণ caption no longer contradicts the button beside it. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc clean, jest **3797/3797 (210 suites)**, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-26 — **fix(questions): a retired question can actually be restored (QR-10, D-#558).** QR-8 shipped the `restoreQuestion` mutation and no path to it — no screen imported `RESTORE_QUESTION`, the `qeRestore`/`qeRetired` labels were never rendered, and the app never asked for `retired: true` — so on production a soft delete was a one-way door for anyone without API access. The bank filter sheet gains a **বাতিলকৃত দেখুন** lens (with its own active-filter chip), a retired row carries a danger **বাতিলকৃত** badge, and opening one shows a notice plus a single **ফেরান** button — edit and mark are withheld, because neither means anything while the question is out of the bank. `retired` is now exposed on the question type and selected by BOTH queries that return it; the detail query was missing it, which would have left the restore button invisible even once built. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3850/3850 (213 suites)**, expo web export exit 0.
- 2026-08-26 — **fix(app): the “বাড়িতে সম্পন্ন হয়েছে” button was only on the day-by-day history, not on the এখনো বাকি list (GC-3).** Found the moment a real guardian opened the portal: the outstanding card is the first thing a parent reads, and the control was below it. `PendingRow` now carries `recordId`/`workId`/`canClaim`/`claim` and renders the same `WorkClaimBlock` — placed OUTSIDE the row’s expand-toggle `Pressable`, since nesting a button in a press target makes the tap ambiguous. App tsc clean, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-26 — **feat(questions): the reviewer can finally narrow her queue (QR-11, D-#559).** 2,951 rounds with only “select all / clear”; the Principal’s publish inbox got filters in QR-6 and hers never did. Her queue now takes the same axes — subject, class, chapter, question type — plus **শুধু বাকিগুলো** (rounds she has not ruled on) and **কেবল গুরুত্বপূর্ণ** (D-#550 marks). The pager’s denominator takes the same filter, so “৫০ / ২৯৫১” can no longer sit above a twelve-row list, and an unfiltered queue keeps the exact `find` it always used — no pipeline, no join. Changing a filter clears the accumulated pages AND the bulk selection, so a bulk verdict can never carry an id the reviewer can no longer see. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3867/3867 (214 suites)** with 14 new, expo web export exit 0.
- 2026-08-26 — **fix(app): the claim button reaches করতে হবে on the guardian home too (GC-3).** The third and last place a parent looks — and the FIRST screen they see. Same `WorkClaimBlock`, outside the row `Pressable` (which navigates away). All three guardian surfaces now render one component from one server-computed `canClaim`. App tsc clean, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-26 — **fix(hr): seven fixes from the second production run of the Staff Hub (SH-10, D-#560–#565).** All seven came from the owner driving the app; none from a test. **Bank details** are now collected with the method that needs them — account number, account holder name, bank and branch for ব্যাংক, the number for বিকাশ, nothing for নগদ — through ONE shared component whose `isBankDetailsComplete` predicate both the wizard and the per-staff pay screen block on, so they cannot drift on what complete means. **পদবি is required on step ১**, because `issueLetter` refuses without it and the failure was landing three screens from the mistake. **The wizard has a back button**, and coming back to step ১ updates the record instead of creating a second one — the half that matters, since step ১ creates on first submit. **The user-facing refusals are in Bangla** (the operator hit an English sentence in an all-Bangla screen; `ChatService` already sets that precedent), while developer-only diagnostics stay English. **The letter screen's "no salary" warning reads the typed value**, having previously contradicted a field the operator had just filled. And **the designation a letter appoints someone to is written back to the profile**, the same defect as the salary write-back (D-#552) one field over — the post existed only inside the PDF. Three of this branch's own tests asserted on the English wording and were updated to the Bangla. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc clean, jest **3853/3853 (213 suites)**, expo web export exit 0.

- 2026-08-26 — **fix(assessment): a retired question can no longer be assembled into a set (QR-12, D-#566).** D-#548 claimed retiring removes a question from “the bank, the assign picker and set assembly” — the third was never implemented. `assertPublished` checked only `reviewStatus`, and retiring does not change it, so a question retired while `gold` sailed through and could still enter a new question paper: exactly the wrong-answer question somebody had just pulled. The guard now refuses `retiredAt != null` too, with its own Bangla reason (বাতিল, not অপ্রকাশিত) so the teacher is not told to go and publish something that was deliberately withdrawn; both `addQuestionToSet` and `createSetWithQuestions` inherit it from the single guard. The app stops offering ঢ়ুড়িতে যোগ on a retired question. Gate: vocab verifier PASS, shared + server + app tsc, server jest **3870/3870 (214 suites)** with 3 new — verified to fail with the guard disabled — expo web export exit 0.
- 2026-08-26 — **fix(trackers): the claim button appeared on rows the server would then REFUSE — `canClaim` did not enforce the 7-school-day window (GC-3, D-#553).** Found by reading a real guardian’s rows on prod: two of five showed a button, and one (`HW-C3-MATH-0017`, 9 open days past due) would have been rejected on tap. `earliestClaimableDueDate` is now a shared helper resolved ONCE per read and reused by `fileWorkClaim`, so the affordance and the guard cannot disagree. Server tsc clean, full jest 3871/3871 (214 suites) — and `homework.test.ts` went 1288s → 13s once the claim model was stubbed there too.

- 2026-08-26 — **chore(decisions): a duplicate-ID index, and one stale row dropped.** Fifteen `D-#` numbers are each used by TWO unrelated decisions — parallel sessions took the next number against a checkout behind `origin/dev`, and append-only merges correctly kept both sides’ CONTENT while silently colliding on the KEY. **Nothing is renumbered:** this file’s own rule is *never rewrite a row; append*, and those IDs are cited by ~250 comments across the codebase plus merged PR descriptions, so renumbering would invalidate every citation to fix an ambiguity a lookup table resolves. The appended index maps each duplicated ID to its two rows by line, date and subject, and records how to avoid the next one (re-check the number when you MERGE dev in, not only when you branch). Separately, the STALE `D-#281` row is dropped — the same decision was recorded twice and the older copy still claimed “PQ-5 NOT YET BUILT” directly below the row saying it shipped, which misinformed rather than duplicated. Docs only; no code, no gate impact.
